# `memoria-patches/` — engine patches for Dream World IX

Dream World IX runs **novel** fields on stock [Memoria](https://github.com/Albeoris/Memoria) and needs a
small **custom engine** only for **forked** fields. The full rationale is in
[`../ff9mapkit/docs/ENGINE.md`](../ff9mapkit/docs/ENGINE.md). These files are unified diffs against a Memoria
source clone — apply the *live* set and compile `Assembly-CSharp`, or just grab the pre-built
`dwix-custom-memoria-<version>.zip` from the GitHub **Releases** page.

This README is the per-file status map (which patches are live, which ship in the bundle, which are dead
history) so a reader isn't left guessing what each file is for.

## Live fork-fidelity set — SHIPPED in the engine bundle

These wrap the hardcoded `fldMapNo == N` engine gates with an *effective field id* so a custom fork inherits
the original field's tuned behavior. This is the set that makes a forked field play faithfully.

| File | What it restores |
|---|---|
| `s23-narrow-map-fork-width.patch` | A forked narrow field gets the donor's exact tuned screen width (no letterbox loss). `s23-narrow-map-fork-width.PR.md` is its upstream write-up. |
| `s24-fork-donor-remap.patch` | The fork→donor remap suite (`EffectiveFieldId` / `ForkSiblingField` / `IsForkField`) — off-mesh exemptions, the fake-battle return field, overworld→fork entry, the no-encounter wrap, and the rest. Folds in the intermediate s25–s28 milestone steps; there is **no separate s25–s28 file**. |
| `s29-fork-donor-softlocks.patch` | The same `EffectiveFieldId` wrap on the remaining late-game (disc 2–4) softlock gates (Iifa / Burmecia / Gulug / Oeilvert / Esto Gaza / Ipsen / Epilogue). Disc-1 gates are in-game proven; these late-disc gates are still being playtested as those zones are forked. |
| `s30-doeventcode-fork-walk.patch` | `EventEngine.DoEventCode.cs` — the file the s24 gate census MISSED. Its per-field event gates use a local alias `Int16 mapNo = fldMapNo` (not the literal `fldMapNo`), so a `grep fldMapNo` census skipped all ~150 `mapNo == N` gates. Adds `Int32 effMapNo = EffectiveFieldId(mapNo)` and routes every gate comparison through it (the 2 non-gate uses — the VoicePlayer arg + `sOriginalFieldNo = mapNo` — stay raw). Restores the per-field scripted-walk destination corrections (the `MOVE`/Walk fixups), e.g. field 53's "Blank jumps in hole" reposition (`destX 250→330`) that puts the falling characters over the hole. ★ IN-GAME PROVEN — fixed the character *positioning* for the 6003 fall (the *occlusion* needed s31). |
| `s31-fork-name-keyed-overlay-offset.patch` | The fork-gate census also missed a whole **NAME-string-keyed** class: `FieldMapExtraOffset` (`mapExtraOffsetList.txt`) tunes per-field overlay z-offsets keyed on the FBG **name**, not `fldMapNo` — so `EffectiveFieldId` (id-based) never reached it. A fork's deployed name (`FBG_N11_PRIM_TSHP_TH_MET`) misses the donor's entry (`FBG_N00_TSHP_MAP006C_TH_MET_4,-1,29,0,-100`), so the floor overlay keeps its raw depth during the fall-move and stops occluding the falling characters. Adds `DataPatchers.EffectiveFieldName(name)` — the name-space analogue of `EffectiveFieldId` (resolves a fork's FBG name → its donor's via the global id↔FBG-name table) — and routes `SetOffset` / `UpdateOverlayOffset` through it on a dict miss. Fixes the WHOLE name-keyed-offset class for every fork, not just 6003. ★ **IN-GAME PROVEN 2026-06-23** — the 6003 jump-into-hole now occludes the falling characters, matching real field 53. |
| `s32-fork-name-gates.patch` | The **rest** of the name-keyed gate class, from a follow-up engine census (the sibling sweep s31 flagged): 7 more sites where engine behavior is keyed on the FBG name a fork doesn't share, wrapped with the same `EffectiveFieldName` lever (generalized in this patch to resolve by the *passed name* so it is correct even at cross-field call sites like the SPS `ChangeFieldOrigin`). **Tier-A (gameplay-breaking on a fork):** the Iifa telescope/harp minigame player- + menu-control unlock (`UIManager.cs`, 2 sites — without it the fork freezes the player), the Conde-Petie space-scene menu unlock (`UIManager.cs`), the Iifa rain-particle world offset (`FieldRainRenderer.cs` — else rain spawns at origin), the Oeilvert star-display opaque-shader override (`BGSCENE_DEF.cs`). **Tier-B:** the per-field SPS sprite z-offsets (`FieldMapSPSExtraOffset.cs`, name-keyed dict like s31) and the Treasure-Hall +8px overlay nudge (`FieldMap.cs`). All identity-safe for real (non-forked) fields. |
| `s33-fork-fldmapno-arg-lookups.patch` | A **THIRD** fork-gate class the censuses missed: `fldMapNo` used as a per-field **LOOKUP ARGUMENT** (not a `== N` comparison like s24/s30, nor a name key like s31/s32). Found via the blank menu **LOCATION** field on a fork. The place-name is looked up by raw field id in `loc_name.mes` (field-id-keyed → a fork's custom id returns empty → blank) at **two** sites: `EventEngine.cs:536` (`mapNameStr`, the debug title) and `MainMenuUI.cs:497` (the in-field menu label — a *separate direct call* the title-bar fix alone missed). Adds `FF9TextTool.FieldLocationName(fldMapNo)` — try the field's **own** id first, else the fork's **donor** — and routes both through it. **Authorable title:** a new `LocationName <id> <title>` DictionaryPatch directive (`DataPatchers.CustomLocationNames`, consulted first by `FieldLocationName`, survives language reloads) lets the kit's `[field] location = "…"` set a from-scratch field's place-name **or override** a fork's inherited donor title. A sweep found 4 sibling argument-lookups, also wrapped: the field→battle **BGM** fallback (`BattleSwirl.cs` + `HonoluluFieldMain.cs` `GetMusicForBattle` — the kit's per-scene BattlePatch override still fires first, so this only changes the *fallback* to the donor), the **mesh-combine** render path (`BGSCENE_DEF.cs` — Crystal World / Iifa Roots / Oeilvert / Pand. Maze), and the **smooth-cam** exclusion (`FieldMap.cs`, ×2). **Deferred** (low value / fiddly): the moogle auto-save eligibility (`IsMoogleField` — gates only the optional `AutoSaveOnlyAtMoogle`, *not* manual save), the cosmetic field-audio preset, the custom-model swap. ★ The menu LOCATION fix is the reported bug; the sweep siblings are IN-GAME UNVERIFIED, verify opportunistically. |

**Area-title gates — investigated, NO fix (a false alarm, resolved 2026-06-23):** the two deferred gates were *not* an asset risk. `FieldMap.SetFieldMapAtlasName` (the `atlas_<lang>` rename) has **zero runtime callers** — dead code — so the missing-texture premise never existed; the runtime atlas load is a flat `Load("atlas")` (`BGSCENE_DEF.cs:839`), and the title lettering is baked into the base `atlas.png` every fork ships (a native Mognet-Central fork renders the card, in-game proven — `FORK_FIDELITY.md`). So English area titles already work on forks (synth forks correctly *hide* them; `--verbatim` shows them via the carried donor `.eb`). `FieldMapLocalizeAreaTitle.GetInfo` only gates the **non-English** `_<lang>.bgs` title-*geometry* swap (`LoadLocalizationInfo` returns early for US), which a native/editable fork can't use without shipping 7 localized `.bgs` sidecars per fork — a cosmetic, non-English-only follow-up, intentionally left (BG-borrow + same-name forks already get it via the donor bundle).

## Custom-overworld (Path C) — NEW capability

| File | What it adds |
|---|---|
| `s39-world-selfheal-control.patch` | **The overworld black-screen SELF-HEAL** (`ff9.w_worldSelfHealControl`, captured from the live tree 2026-07-15). Recovers the "no controlled actor" state (`project-ff9-overworld-actor-brick`): editing overworld geometry under a saved spawn leaves the leader with no `wmActor` / no control designator → `ff9.GetControlChar()` null → `w_frameUpdate` keeps `w_moveActorPtr` on the DUMMY → the camera has nothing to follow → **BLACK, baked into the save**. Called from `w_frameUpdate` case 0 ONLY when `GetControlChar()` is null; rebuilds the control body, ensures every active world actor has a `wmActor` (`w_movementChrInitSlice` dereferences it with no null check), force-grounds via a sky-cast, and binds the camera actor. **SELF-TERMINATES** (once it binds a real actor the else-branch never fires again — no one-shot flag) and is try/catch-wrapped so recovery can never crash the frame loop. Never touches the Bee scene. ✅ The author's TODO on the degenerate-spawn fallback is RESOLVED (verified with the kit's engine-faithful ground query, `world/placement.py`, against stock disc-1 blocks): `kWorldSafeFallback` (768, −640) — the map centre — IS open sea; it sits on the corner of blocks [11][9]/[12][9]/[11][10]/[12][10], three of which do not exist (open sea) and the fourth grounds on Sea4 (topograph 57). Replacement chosen and verified walkable: **(1092, −788)** — Block[17][12], the Dali plain (the first overworld ground a new game walks), a single walkable sheet, ground_y 11.4, dominant plains topograph 49, gentle slope, ~30u clear of the Dali entrance trigger. The constant fix lands with the b18 bundle rebuild. |
| `s34-worldmap-mesh-override.patch` | Load a world-map block TERRAIN mesh from a loose **`.ff9mesh`** file in a mod folder instead of the baked Resources mesh — so overworld geometry can be edited **without** an AssetBundle repack (FF9 is Unity 5.2.3; `AssetManager.LoadFromDisc<Mesh>` is unsupported, so there is no other loose-mesh hook). New class `Memoria.World.WorldMeshOverride` (reads the kit's [`ff9mapkit.world.mesh`](../ff9mapkit/ff9mapkit/world/mesh.py) format) + a hook in **`WMWorld.RegisterBlockComponent`** — the RUNTIME block-stream path. ⚠ The first cut hooked `WMWorldPrefabMaker.LoadMesh`, which is reachable only from `LoadModelAsset` — an **editor-only prefab-builder with ZERO runtime callers** (the overworld loads a *pre-built* WorldDisc prefab and streams blocks via `WMWorld.LoadBlock`), so it never fired. The live hook reads `block.InitialX/InitialY` (grid coords) + `transform.name` to build the override path, and swaps the `MeshFilter.sharedMesh` (render) **and** the `mesh` fed to `WMBlock.AddWalkMesh` (collision + the `tangent.x` entry id) — one object. **The override is GENERIC over `transform.name`, not terrain-only**: any part (`Terrain` / `Object` / `Sea1..6` / `Beach1`) that has a component to override takes this path, and for `ObjectForm1` the loose mesh IS fed to the Form1 walkmesh (`AddWalkMeshForm1`), registered BEFORE `TerrainForm1` — so a baked Object override SHADOWS the terrain in the ground query unless its triangles carry a `WMPhysics`-skipped IDALL (4078/4088/2040). **Also carries `RegisterBareObjectOverride`**: a block with a `TerrainForm1` but NO stock `ObjectForm1` gets an Object child created from the terrain template so a loose `Object` `.ff9mesh` renders at all — that one is render-only on purpose (`AddForm1Transform`, no `AddWalkMeshForm1`), since a whole 3D building mesh as a collider turns its back-face-culled walls + buried base into invisible collision. Identity-safe: no override file → stock mesh. **Also add `<Compile Include="Memoria\World\WorldMeshOverride.cs" />` to the csproj** — ⚠ that Compile line currently lives in **`s22`**, misattributed but load-bearing (it is the only patch adding it); moving it here is deferred while the csproj is in flight (see STACK HEALTH). **Also carries the custom-ocean loose-TEXTURE override** (recaptured from the live tree 2026-07-15, previously in no patch): `WorldMeshOverride.TryLoadTexture` + a render hook in `RegisterBlockComponent` that clones the material and swaps `mainTexture` when a loose custom texture exists for a cell+part, **keeping the sea shader** — so a custom ocean renders as a continuous gradient instead of discrete tile shades. Cell-scoped; identity-safe (no override file → stock material). ★ Built + deployed 2026-06-30 (re-hooked + rebuilt clean); ★ IN-GAME PROVEN by continuous use — the dedicated `world-deploy --spike` playtest was never run as such, but every custom-overworld playtest since rides this override: the Southern Ring's 56 reclaimed disc-1 cells and the Path D synthetic-disc rounds all load through it (`studies/path-d-new-world/RUNGS-0-3-PLAYTEST.md`: those cells are "the already-in-game-proven path"; `DRAFT.md`: the reclaim/donor route is "in-game-proven as of s34"). The foundation for Path C (geometry-edit the real world) and Path D (a minted continent's geometry). → `project-ff9-worldmap-feasibility`. |

**s34's vertex bound is CORRECT — no engine change.** `ReadMesh` (`WorldMeshOverride.cs:186`) admits
`vcount <= 65535` (16-bit indices). The offline claim that Unity 5.2.3p2 refuses a Mesh over 65000 vertices
(CAP-1) was **refuted in-game**: 65,001- and 65,535-vertex Terrain overrides render and walk
(`studies/terrain-malleability/ingame/RESULTS.md` §8). The kit's `mesh.MAX_MESH_VERTS` equals the loader's
65535, pinned by `test_world_ledger.py::test_engine_patch_literals_are_pinned`.

## A genuinely THIRD overworld (Path D) — NEW capability, ★ PROVEN IN-GAME (the 9013 field↔world round trip owner-confirmed 2026-08-05 → `studies/path-d-new-world/rung6/PLAYTEST.md`)

Rungs 0–2 of [`../studies/path-d-new-world/PLAN.md`](../studies/path-d-new-world/PLAN.md). All three built +
deployed together 2026-07-29 (0 errors, both arches, `Output\Assembly-CSharp.dll` == both deployed copies,
sha `B60003A67130A90A…`; pre-build full DLL backup **`20260729-153010`** → `py tools/restore_memoria_dll.py
20260729-153010`). **Every one of them is INERT until deliberately armed**, so a normal disc-1/4 load — and
every other concurrent worktree sharing this install — is unaffected. Gate: the three patches apply forward
in order (`patch -p1 --binary`) onto pristine snapshots and reproduce the live tree **byte-for-byte** on all
five touched files. ⚠ Bundling note: three patches, but each in-game test still changes exactly ONE variable
(`s72` does nothing without a `WorldScene` DictionaryPatch line; `s71` does nothing until its toggle is
armed) — the bundling buys one relaunch instead of three, it does not merge the success signals.

| File | What it adds |
|---|---|
| `s70-debug-menu-reach-widen.patch` | **THE REACHABILITY FIX (Rung 0) — the prerequisite nothing else could be observed without.** `Ff9mkDebugMenu.ForceWorldState` hard-rejected any id outside `9000-9012`, and it is the ONLY route to a chosen `wldMapNo`: the other candidate, `WMBeeMenu`'s "Jump To", is a same-world POSITION teleport whose host `Bee` object `WMScriptDirector.HonoAwake` destroys on every world load — doubly dead. Widened to `1-32767` (`wldMapNo` is `Int16` and `ArmWorldReload`'s cast is unguarded, so a larger number would silently wrap onto a different, possibly REAL dispatcher). The engine's own dispatch path never had this gate: `ff9InitStateWorldMap` just does `FF9DBAll.EventDB[MapNo]`. Also adds a `Memoria.Prime.Log.Message` line — **the only channel that reaches `Memoria.log`; plain `Debug.Log` does not** (no Unity→Memoria bridge exists; this file's three existing `[ff9mk]` `Debug.Log` calls are dead-lettered, and the plan's stated verify channel was therefore unreadable). ⚠ `ArmWorldReload` has two preconditions the plan did not price: `UIManager.State == WorldHUD` **and** `sys.mode == 3` — it can only be fired while already standing on an overworld. Worth keeping permanently; every later rung needs it. |
| `s71-worlddisc-runtime-spike.patch` | **THE WORLDDISC SPIKE (Rung 1) — THROWAWAY, the pivotal unknown of the whole plan** (`s63`/`s67` house style: remove or re-gate once it has answered). New `Memoria/World/WorldDiscSpike.cs` (+ csproj include) mints a complete 24×20 `WMBlock` grid in C# at runtime and assigns it to `WMWorld.WorldDisc`, hooked at the top of `Initialize()` **before** the `:105` `BuildBlockArray` — `InitialBlocks` and `Blocks` must come from the SAME hierarchy or `Block31/115/219`, `ResetBlockForms` and the `ff9.cs` grid lookups silently address the wrong one. There is no smaller version: `OnInitialize`'s position loop walks all 480 cells with no null guard, so day one needs a complete gap-free grid. Armed from a **session-only, default-OFF** debug-menu toggle (the `_vehicleForceAll` idiom) rather than an `.ini` flag — an ini flag persists and costs a relaunch between Rungs 0 and 1; a toggle is off on every launch, which matters on an install shared by ~26 concurrent worktrees. **Three landmines the plan's own sketch would have died on, all found by re-reading source and all fixed here:** (1) `Form2Transforms` is declared with no initializer and `LoadBlock` re-creates only THREE of the four collections while `ApplyForm()` foreaches it unconditionally — all four are now assigned (free: `LoadBlock` overwrites three moments later); (2) `CurrentX`/`CurrentY` are read by `GetAbsoluteBlock`/`SwapBlock`/`DetectUnseenBlocks` and stock seeds them alongside `Initial*` — left 0, every lookup returns cell (0,0); (3) with no dispatcher there is no player, so `w_moveActorPtr` stays the origin-parked dummy and `DetectUnseenBlocks` indexes `Blocks[0,-1]` → `IndexOutOfRangeException` **that has nothing to do with the substitution** and would be misread as the spike failing — the sentinel is now parked mid-grid at (12,10) so its ±8 window stays in bounds. ⚠ The plan's "all cells sea, no override lookups" premise is **refuted by the live install**: 56 disc-1 `Terrain.ff9mesh` overrides + `Donor.txt` sidecars already ship in `FF9CustomMap-world`, so 56 of 480 cells take the s34 reclaim path. Deliberately NOT suppressed — those cells are the already-proven path, and an ocean containing only the Southern Ring's reclaimed cells is an unmistakable success picture. |
| `s73-third-worlddisc-wire.patch` | **THE PERMANENT WIRE (Rung 3→5).** Promotes `s71` from a manual debug toggle to an automatic per-id wire: `WorldDiscSpike.Latch(wldMapNo)` engages a synthetic disc for any id in the **reserved Path D band 9013-9099** (verified clear across both live `DictionaryPatch.txt` files apart from 9013 itself), or via the session-only toggle for experiments on other ids. `Latch()` ALWAYS assigns, so an engaged load can never leak into the next real disc-1/4 load, and it latches ONCE per load into `Engaged` so every downstream consumer (the `s74` tag, the special-object guard) agrees even if the toggle is flipped mid-load. Reads `FF9StateSystem.Common.FF9.wldMapNo`, which is already populated when `WMWorld.Initialize()` runs (`WMScriptDirector.HonoAwake` sets it upstream), wrapped defensively. `s71` is retired-not-deleted per the `s63`/`s67` house style — its patch file stays as history and its debug toggle stays as the manual override. |
| `s74-sentinel-disc-override-namespace.patch` | **THE OVERRIDE NAMESPACE (Rung 5 prerequisite).** New `private Int32 overrideDiscTag` on `WMWorld` (⚠ `private`, no `[SerializeField]` — `WMWorld` is baked into a prefab and ANY new serialized field shifts its layout into a corrupt deserialize/black screen; same reason `currentDisc` is private), assigned in `Initialize()` once `currentDisc` is final and gated on `WorldDiscSpike.Engaged`, so real play is byte-identical. Threaded through the **six** `WorldMeshOverride` lookups across five methods — `LoadBlock`'s `HasLandOverride`, `ResolveReclaimDonor`'s `TryReadDonorPath`, `RegisterBlockComponent`'s mesh + texture lookups, `RegisterBareObjectOverride`, and `UpdateLoadBlocks`' streaming mirror. **Two corrections to the plan's own design, both verified by direct read.** (1) It said `RegisterBlockComponent`/`RegisterBareObjectOverride` need new parameters — they do NOT: both are instance methods already reading `this.currentDisc`, and the parameter-threading alternative would have needed THREE signature changes, not two. (2) It said `WorldMeshOverride.cs` needs **zero** change — **false, and a silent-failure trap**: `TryReadDonorPath` spends its single `disc` argument on two different jobs, the loose `Donor.txt` override key (`:94`) AND a REAL `WorldMap/Prefabs/WorldDisc{disc}/…` Resources path (`:112`). A sentinel threaded through both resolves every donor to a nonexistent `WorldDisc9` prefab → `AssetManager.Load` returns null → `ResolveReclaimDonor` falls back to the plain inland donor, dropping every beach/sea/foam carry **with nothing logged**. Split into a 4-arg `TryReadDonorPath(overrideDisc, assetDisc, x, y)` with a 3-arg overload so every existing caller is untouched. Sentinel disc = **9** (safe: `w_frameDisc` is only ever 0/1/4 and the only `WorldMap/Disc<N>` folders on disk are 1 and 4). **Also carries the special-object guard** — the four ACTIVE `block.Number` cases (219 Water Shrine, 389 the Daguerreo bridge prefab, 91 + 115 quicksand; the other six cases are empty stubs) are suppressed on a synthetic disc, so a new continent does not inherit stock landmarks just for reusing their grid coordinates. Found by an owner sailing the all-sea 9013 world → `studies/path-d-new-world/INHERITED-STOCK-BEHAVIOR.md`. Built + deployed both arches, `Output` == both deployed copies; pre-build backup **`20260729-165601`**. ★ IN-GAME PROVEN — s75's Rung 5a close rode this namespace (CLONE mode resolves per-cell overrides through it, owner-confirmed 2026-07-30), and rung 6 sealed the arc: the 9013 field↔world round trip owner-confirmed 2026-08-05 ("! showed and warped me back, the loop works") → `studies/path-d-new-world/rung6/PLAYTEST.md`. |
| `s75-worlddisc-clone-mode-and-mist.patch` | **CLONE-vs-BLANK MODE + MIST SUPPRESSION (Rung 5, owner decisions 2026-07-29).** Three session-only statics on `WorldDiscSpike`: **`CloneStockWorld`** (default false = BLANK, a true 480-all-sea empty canvas; true = copy the STOCK world's per-cell flags off the real baked hierarchy so the synthetic grid renders the stock world map while still resolving per-cell overrides through the s74 Path-D namespace — the owner's "reshape a stock world without touching it" non-destructive sandbox); **`SuppressMist`** (default true — "the new continent is CLEAN OF MIST"; read by `WorldConfiguration.UseMist()`; NOT purely cosmetic, `w_frameFog` is part of the encounter lookup key); **`SuppressStockLandmarks`** (getter `Engaged && !CloneStockWorld` — a BLANK world suppresses the four cell-keyed stock landmark cases (219 Water Shrine / 389 Daguerreo bridge / 91+115 quicksand) it would otherwise inherit for reusing their grid coordinates, a CLONE deliberately keeps them, and off a synthetic disc it is always false so real play is untouched). ★ In-game confirmed 2026-07-30 with the Rung 5a close: CLONE mode carves the island into the same cells (F2), mist suppression toggles live (F3). |
| `s72-worldscene-directive.patch` | **THE `WorldScene` DIRECTIVE (Rung 2)** — the `FieldScene`/`BattleScene` sibling for the world lane, in `DataPatchers.cs`. `WorldScene <id> <NAME> [mesID]` writes `FF9DBAll.EventDB[id] = "EVT_WORLD_" + NAME`. **Registers `EventEngineUtils.eventIDToMESID[id]` too — a second unguarded indexer the plan missed entirely**: `ff9ShutdownStateFieldMap` reads it when a field EXITS to the world map, firing while still in the old field, before the world scene even loads. Defaults to mesID **68**, the shared world text block every stock world id maps to; reusing it deliberately avoids minting a new message-file id, which would shadow some real location's dialogue. Inert until a `DictionaryPatch.txt` actually carries a `WorldScene` line. ⚠ EventDB is ONE flat namespace shared with `FieldScene`/`BattleScene`, last-write-wins with no collision check — a world dispatcher id must be unique across every stacked mod folder, not just among world ids. |

## Multiplayer co-op (experimental) — NEW capability

| File | What it adds |
|---|---|
| `s36-netsync-ghost.patch` | **Exploration co-op "ghost sync"** — two players see each other walk a shared field in real time. New namespace `Memoria.Netsync`: `NetSyncClient` (a `DontDestroyOnLoad` MonoBehaviour bootstrapped from `HonoluluFieldMain.HonoAwake`) + **two transports behind `INetTransport`**: `NetSyncSocket` (direct LAN TCP host/client, background read/write threads, lock-guarded latest-state slots, staleness timeout) and `NetSyncRelay` (a hand-rolled RFC-6455 WebSocket **client** for the public rendezvous relay — handshake, client-masked binary frames, ping/pong, reconnect, a 75s pre-pairing receive window matched to the relay's 60s pair timeout); plus one `HonoAwake` line and three csproj `<Compile Include>`s. Reads a `[Netsync]` section from `Memoria.ini` (`Enabled` / `Role`=selftest\|host\|client / `PeerAddress` / `Port` / `TargetField` / `RelayUrl` / `SessionCode` / `InsecureTls`); `RelayUrl` set → relay transport, empty → direct TCP; **OFF by default** (`Enabled=0` → nothing is created, zero footprint on a normal game). **`TargetField = 0` (the default) = co-op EVERYWHERE** — the transport dials once and lives for the whole session, each side broadcasts its true location every frame (a field-0 sentinel during menus/battle/transitions — which also fixed a latent bug where the 30 Hz keepalive re-sent a stale field id and FROZE the departing player's ghost on the peer's screen), and ghosts appear on any screen both players share (`gMode==1` — battle and the overworld are out of scope); a nonzero `TargetField` restricts to that one field (the original hangout mode). The overlay names the peer's current field ("your friend is on another screen — field N"). ★ Everywhere-mode solo tier IN-GAME PROVEN 2026-07-12 (selftest ghost follows into stock fields; nothing on the world map or in battle; clean reconnect on field re-entry); the cross-machine bits (peer-elsewhere overlay, leave-a-field despawn) await the next two-machine session. A relay **host with no `SessionCode` auto-generates a random namespaced `ff9-XXXXXXXX` code and persists it into `Memoria.ini`** (the code is the session's access gate; a guest with no code refuses to dial). **`[Netsync]` HOT-RELOADS**: the client watches `Memoria.ini` (mtime-gated file stat every ~2s + a parsed-value signature, so its own code-persist isn't an "edit") and applies changes to a RUNNING game — transport redialed, ghost/overlay reset; enabling co-op from OFF applies at the next field load (nothing is created while disabled). The relaunch-per-edit rule is dead for this section (★ in-game proven 2026-07-12: enabling from off appeared at the next field transition; a live disable vanished the overlay in ~2s, no relaunch). ⚠ The game's old Mono has no TLS 1.2, so `RelayUrl` should point at the local [`tools/netsync-bridge/`](../tools/netsync-bridge/README.md) (`ws://127.0.0.1:49201`), which carries the connection to the relay over TLS. On a shared field it spawns the peer as a "ghost" **wearing the peer's OWN model** (wire v2, 38 bytes: `{field,anim,model,meshVis,pos,rot}` — the model id resolves through `FF9BattleDB.GEO` exactly like the event engine spawns actors, re-dresses live when the peer changes character, and falls back to Zidane ONLY when the id can't resolve/load, e.g. a modded character this install lacks; the peer's own 16-bit per-submesh visibility mask drives sheathed weapons etc., replacing the old local-player index-mirror; v1 peers are length- **and** version-rejected → mixed engine versions silently don't sync). The model recipe is `FieldMap.AddPlayer` minus the controller — renderers enabled + masked, `GeoTexAnim` texture anims, huge skinned-mesh bounds so the PSX-shader-projected model isn't frustum-culled — and the transform + animation interpolate with exponential smoothing (`LerpRate=12`) to hide network jitter (anim ids are global, so the peer's clips bind natively on their rig — the upside-down-Zidane bug is dead). ★ Non-Zidane ghosts solo tier IN-GAME PROVEN 2026-07-12 (a correct right-side-up Garnet mirror, re-dress on a mid-session character swap, Zidane regression clean); cross-machine awaits the next two-machine session. NO field/battle/story-state sync (each save is independent). ★ Direct-TCP **IN-GAME PROVEN 2026-07-08** — two machines over WiFi, each rendering the other's live Zidane ghost moving + animating. ★ Relay path **IN-GAME PROVEN 2026-07-11** — two machines each through a local bridge to the public relay over TLS, paired by an auto-generated session code; the ghost appeared ~1 s after field load (`test_relay_roundtrip.py` re-proves the bridge+relay path offline, without the game). An **on-screen status overlay** shows the session code + pairing state in game — "waiting for your friend… code ff9-XXXX" while unpaired, a bridge-not-running hint if the transport can't connect (`INetTransport.IsConnected`), an F6-warp nudge when co-op is on but you're in another field — and hides the moment the peer's ghost is up (★ in-game proven 2026-07-12 — including cross-version pairing: an overlay-less peer DLL paired fine, the wire protocol is unchanged). **Ghost lighting** (the last cosmetic gap, closed): registered characters get a flat per-char `_Color` tint each frame — `charClr(model) + lightClr(floor)` from the field's `mapconfig` (`fldmcf.ff9fieldMCFService` computes, `fldchar` applies) — which the ghost, living outside the char table, never received; the ghost now replicates the same math at spawn (its OWN model's char color + the LOCAL player's floor light, default light as fallback) — ★ lighting IN-GAME PROVEN 2026-07-12 (the selftest mirror matches the scene's shading). No known cosmetic gaps remain. → `project-ff9-multiplayer-injector`. |
| `s37-netsync-battle.patch` | **Battle co-op, rungs B0+B1 (EXPERIMENTAL — applies on top of s36)** — the "networked controller 2" design from `studies/battle-coop/`: the battle always runs on ONE machine; the peer spectates it live and (optionally) commands assigned party slots. **Wire v3**: every message becomes a typed frame `[magic][ver=3][type][len][payload]` on both transports — type 0 position (the old 36-byte payload, latest-slot + keepalive), type 1 battle state (latest-slot), type 2 battle commands (**FIFO, never collapsed** — the TCP/WS streams are already reliable+ordered), type 3 control; v2 peers are version-rejected (the established mixed-versions-don't-sync fail-safe; update both machines). New `NetSyncBattle` (+ csproj include): **B0** — in battle, a ~150 ms sampler serializes the roster (name markup stripped, logical-HP accessors) into type-1 frames; the peer renders a left-side OnGUI panel (enemies numbered, party HP/MP/ATB%, ready markers) and the idle overlay quiets while it shows. **B1** — `[Netsync] GuestSlots = <bitmask of party slots 0-3>` (default 0 = spectate-only) offers those slots to the peer: the local HUD skips them (guards in the ready loop, `SwitchPlayer`, the two switch-rotation paths), the peer's panel turns into a one-keystroke command menu on their turn (`[1-8]` = Attack the Nth listed enemy, `[0]` = Defend), and the arriving command frame is validated (guest slot, alive, ATB-ready, `cmd[0]` free; dead target → first live enemy) then queued through a new `BattleHUD.SendNetCommand` — modeled verbatim on the engine's own `SendAutoAttackCommand`/`SendCommand` (duplicate guard, `sel_mode=1`, `InputFinishList`, blink clear; never touches `CurrentPlayerIndex`). **ATB feel**: a `RemoteMenuOpen` control flag (staleness-guarded, ~2.5 s decay — a hung/disconnected guest can never freeze the host) is OR'd into `IsNativeEnableAtb` (vanilla WAIT) and the Turn-Based `isMenuing` — so Wait/Turn-Based hosts get their native gauge-freeze during remote turns while Active/Dynamic stay untouched; on guest stale/disconnect the slots revert to local control (the skip guards read the same liveness). **Selftest = the solo proof**: with `Role=selftest` + `GuestSlots` set, your own battle loops back through the FULL codec — the panel appears over the real fight and the guest slots are commanded by the assist keys instead of their menu (PS1 2-player battle, played solo with both hands). Assist digits are SWALLOWED from the game's key handlers while the panel awaits a command (`SwallowAssistKey`, an F6-pattern early intercept in `UIKeyTrigger.Update` — found in playtest: Alpha1 is Memoria's default help/Select binding and double-fired). ★ SOLO TIER IN-GAME PROVEN 2026-07-12 (panel live, keypress commands, WAIT-mode gauge freeze on remote turns, kill-renumber retarget, clean digits after the swallow fix). **ABILITIES + ITEMS (wire v4, same day, ★ solo-proven):** a type-4 ROSTER frame streams each guest slot's menu content (learned-ability ids + MP + live enabled state + target type, battle-usable items + counts — ids only, names resolve from the guest's own install) built by `BattleHUD.CollectNetMenus` (mirrors DisplayAbility/GetAbilityState/DisplayItem per slot); the guest panel is a digit-menu state machine (Root → paginated ability/item lists → target pick; All/Self/Random auto-resolve; ForDead abilities list the DEAD — Phoenix Down proven); the host re-resolves the command per slot (trance-aware) and revalidates LIVE at injection (`NetAbilityUsable`; closed a real unlearned-cast cheat path found in playtest — an empty-list single-shot now requires the first USABLE ability). **`[Netsync] GuestWaitMs`** (default 30s, 0=unlimited) caps an idle guest's Wait/Turn-Based gauge freeze per turn — a fallback, not a rush timer; the host adjusts. Excluded v1: Throw, double-cast/mix, monster-transform. **VISITOR MODE (wire v5, same day; ⚠ dressing+suppression solo-provable, follow-warp two-machine-only):** the stock-play vision — the guest is a presence in the HOST's game. New `NetSyncVisitor` (+ csproj include): (a) **ghost dressing** — `[Netsync] GhostAs = auto|<playable>` dresses the PEER's ghost as the party member of my first GuestSlots bit (the character they command in battle is the character they appear as); anim ids are rig-partitioned, so the pos frame gains a sender-classified **rig-neutral loco byte** (idle/walk/run/turnL/turnR/other, classified against the sender's own rig via the kit's proven home-field clip table, 8 mains; the receiver resolves it on the dressed rig — gestures fall back to idle; the wire submesh mask is skipped when dressed, it's only index-valid on the peer's own model); (b) **`FollowHost = 1`** (guest side) — auto-warp my game to the peer's broadcast field id, debounced 1.2s against door transitions, registration-guarded (`eventIDToFBGID` — an unregistered field shows "your friend is on field N, which this install doesn't have" instead of a black screen), using the exact F6 deferred-fade warp recipe; (c) **encounter suppression** — while following a connected host (or selftest), `SettingsState.IsNoEncounter` ORs in `NetSyncVisitor.SuppressEncounters` (one line; the booster property all three encounter sites already consult) so only the host's battles exist; link down → normal encounters instantly. ★ VISITOR SOLO TIER IN-GAME PROVEN 2026-07-12 (dressing, party-order re-dress, GhostAs hot-reload cycling incl. auto↔explicit, encounter pause + hot-reload, dressed-ghost lighting incl. per-frame light-zone tracking, mask parity "no dagger", and the camera clean through reorders). The proof round fixed three real bugs: dressed-ghost lighting FALLBACK (a dressed model has no entry in the host field's mapconfig → now worn-model → the LOCAL PLAYER's entry → default) and CONTINUITY (the engine re-tints registered chars per frame; the ghost now re-applies at ~7 Hz, riding the local player's floor light), and — the big one — **the HonoBehavior teardown law**: the ghost's `GeoTexAnim` is a `HonoBehavior` (self-registers at Awake), and `HonoBehaviorSystem.Update` SWEEPS its list (`IsEnabled()`) BEFORE processing queued removals — so a raw `Object.Destroy` of a HonoBehavior-carrying object MID-SCENE leaves one tick that dereferences the destroyed component → a per-frame NRE that aborts the sweep forever and freezes every later HonoBehavior (the field camera pipeline; found via a frozen camera after a party reorder re-dressed the ghost mid-field). Sanctioned teardown: `UnregisterHonoBehavior(dispose: true)` — the system removes every child behavior synchronously and destroys the GameObject itself. Field-transition despawns never hit this (the system rebuilds per scene), which is why it lurked. FollowHost's warp remains the one two-machine-only untested piece. **THE COOP CELLS (V2 `[[coop]]` authoring; ★ SOLO-PROVEN 2026-07-12 — the two-plate gate fired ONCE on step-in at field 4003, proving the broadcast, the expression compare, AND the once-latch in one report):** while Enabled, every frame writes the PEER's presence + walkmesh position into kit-reserved `gEventGlobal` bytes 2032-2039 (`WriteCoopCells`: presence byte @2032, X/Z Int16 LE @2034/2036 — byte-addressed exactly as `EBin.GetVariableValueInternal` reads, beside the kit's mask scratch at 2040) — kit-compiled `[[coop]]` two-plate gates read them as ordinary GLOB vars in region Range bodies (engine = mechanism, `.eb` = policy; the engine knows nothing about plates). Never written unless Enabled (a vanilla save is untouched); presence zeroes on unpair/out-of-scope/config-change. Selftest writes the mirror's position (+250 x), so a gate with plates 250 apart is solo-provable. **STATE-MIRROR RUNG 1 (wire v5→v6, regenerated into this patch 2026-07-15):** the authoritative-host substrate — a type-5 STATE frame carries the HOST's `gEventGlobal` (section-tagged payload `[id u8][len u16 LE][bytes]`; section 0 = the 2048-byte story heap; the coop cells 2032-2041 are masked BOTH ways so the guest keeps its own `[[coop]]` view), published ~7 Hz by the host; a FOLLOWING guest applies it at the field-load boundary (`HonoluluFieldMain.ff9InitStateFieldMap` → `NetSyncClient.ApplyStoryBeforeEvents()` immediately before `StartEvents`) so its fields stage the HOST's story instead of scenario-zero. New `NetSyncState.cs` (+ csproj include): snapshot/apply (`ApplyStoryTo` is scratch-targetable) + capture/restore + a selftest that round-trips the REAL wire codec (`BuildFrame`/`TryParseHeader` → the real section-walk apply into a scratch buffer, never the live array). **Session save-safety (two guards + an exit ramp):** while mirroring, the guest can neither manual-save (`SaveLoadUI.OnKeyConfirm` refuses on `NetSyncClient.IsMirroringStory`) nor autosave (the `EventEngine` guard below), so the guest's DISK stays pristine all session; and a session that ENDS exits through **the autoload ramp** (`NetSyncState.ExitMirrorToOwnSave`): the guest's OWN autosave is reloaded — the verbatim moogle-menu load transition; no autosave yet → title screen — NEVER an in-place flag restore, which would leave the guest's own story standing at the HOST's location (a sequence-break/softlock frankenstate the base game can't produce). Link-drop arms the ramp debounced (3s, relay-blip-proof); a config change arms it immediately; the mirroring flag stays true until the ramp fires so the save-block stays armed through the gap; the ramp fires only free-standing on a field/world map; app teardown skips it (the disk is already coherent). (Kit-side, `coop host` forces `FollowHost=0`.) Two battle-menu fixes ride the same round: `CollectNetMenus` SKIPS an Ability command with zero learned abilities (no more empty "Swd Art" → dead target prompt on the guest), and `BuildRoster` waits quietly via the new `BattleHUD.NetMenusReady` probe until `InitialBattle()`→`ManageAbility()` seeds the ability dict — the battle-intro `KeyNotFoundException`/`roster build failed` log spam (pre-existing since B1) is gone, same wire behavior. v5-and-older peers are version-rejected; NOTE the transport asymmetry: `NetSyncSocket` logs+disconnects on a version mismatch, the relay just never pairs — check both DLLs before debugging "no sync". ★ SOLO TIER PROVEN 2026-07-15 (selftest codec OK ×3 launches, Sword Art absent in-game, zero roster errors + a guest Attack through the quiet path). **★ TWO-MACHINE PROVEN same day (Phase 6): fail-safe (v5+v6 never pair), RENDER-MATCH (field 354 rotating cast re-stages from the host's ScenarioCounter — the authoritative-host headline), Twin Altar incl. the held door, battle B0+B1, follow-warp.** The session also found the last save-safety hole: **AUTOSAVE bypasses the SaveLoadUI block** — `EventEngine`'s field-load `serializer.Autosave` writes the Continue slot on every transition, so a following guest's Continue collected the host's mirrored story + the followed-to field. Fixed in this patch (`EventEngine.cs` joins the set, 16 files): `noSave \|= NetSyncVisitor.SuppressEncounters \|\| NetSyncClient.IsMirroringStory` — a FOLLOWING guest never autosaves (encounter-suppression's exact scoping: following + connected, or selftest; vanilla on link-drop; hosts and non-following guests keep vanilla autosave). Save-safety re-test = the remaining Phase 6 box (`studies/battle-coop/PLAN.md`). |
| `s38-netsync-spectator-field.patch` | **ROAD A — SPECTATOR-STRICT (applies on top of s37)** — the correctness half of the SPECTATOR-FIELD paradigm: *a following co-op guest has no interaction authority, no local state authority, and no local escape.* Closes **a live, present-tense exploit**: the guest's flags are mirror-owned and TRANSIENT (all 2048 gEventGlobal bytes overwritten from the host on every field load) while its bag is guest-owned and PERSISTENT (the mirror never touches `FF9.item`) — so a guest opening a chest the host hasn't looted banks the item in its REAL bag, the next field load reverts the "opened" flag from the host's snapshot, and the chest is closed again → **an unbounded item farm, one item per field re-entry**. Three gates, all keyed on `NetSyncClient.IsMirroringStory` (the same predicate as the save gates; its "arming gap" — false until the first mirrored field load — is CORRECT, since until then the guest's own story is still authoritative): (1) **`AchievementManager.ProcessAchievementReport`** — the guest's stray writes are all discarded by the autoload exit ramp *except* a Steam achievement report, which leaves the machine and no ramp can retract; this is the single funnel every channel (item/battle/ability/minigame) crosses before the native call. Do NOT gate `FF9Item_Achievement` instead — `ITEMADD` fires five `EMinigame` achievements that bypass it. (2) **ENCOUNT `0x2A` + ENCOUNT2 `0x8C`** (`DoEventCode`) — a mirrored script was booting a LOCAL battle the host isn't in, whose result screen writes the guest's real gil + item drops + Steal; `SuppressEncounters` never covered these (it reaches only `ff9.IsNoEncounter` = the random-step and worldmap rolls). Returns **0** (inline continue), and **the guard MUST sit below every `getv`** — `commandDefault2` branches on `gArgUsed` and REWINDS the IP one byte if no arg was consumed, re-executing the opcode forever. (3) **the menu** — all four `UIKeyTrigger` `IsMenuControlEnable` reads, incl. the Alt+F2 party / Alt+F5-F9 save-load shortcuts that bypass `OnKeyMenu` entirely. Gated at the READ, **not** via an `IsMenuControlEnable` getter: `FieldMapActorController:167` reads that flag to decide an INACTIVE actor should idle, so forcing it false drops those actors into movement processing. **Deliberately NOT blocked:** tread regions (tag 2) — the `[[coop]]` plate gates and the Twin Altar's held door live there, and a gateway only moves the guest (follow-warp overrides it, the ramp discards location); and dialogue/`B_KEYON`, which the guest needs to advance its own mirrored cutscenes. **No wire change (still v7)** — so a stale peer still pairs: **the gates are guest-side, so BOTH machines need this DLL for symmetric protection.** ★ SOLO REGRESSION + TWO-MACHINE PROVEN 2026-07-15 (menus refuse incl. both Alt lanes; Evil Forest Plant Brain + plant-spider scripted fights skipped on the guest while the host fought; no regressions in coop gates, battle B0/B1, cutscenes, or the exit ramp). **Known residue (rung 2, not shipped):** a real chest on a *verbatim fork* is proximity-dispatched with its confirm read INSIDE the script (`B_KEYON`), so it still opens — but post-A the item lands in a bag nothing reads and the ramp discards, i.e. theater, not corruption. Closing it needs a pad mask (risks wedging any script that polls a button) → `studies/battle-coop/inventory-authority.md`. |

| `s54-netsync-diorama-silent.patch` | **THE HOST-SILENT DIORAMA (captured 2026-07-23; wire UNCHANGED at v10; 4 files; ★ two-machine proven 2026-07-23).** Stops a following guest’s own field/world BGM bleeding through a diorama battle when the HOST is audibly silent. Pre-s54 the `0xFFFE` host-silent sentinel folded to `songid = -1`, which hit `BattleSwirl`’s `songid == -1` early return with `IsPlayFieldBGMInCurrentBattle` left TRUE (the guest’s own music kept playing), and `NetSyncDiorama`’s pre-swirl seam SUSPENDED the field BGM (a suspend-slot the guest’s own field reload re-stacked, doubling the music across boot→leave→re-boot). Now: `BattleSwirl`’s two Request handlers (field + world) render TRUE silence on `0xFFFE` (`IsPlayFieldBGMInCurrentBattle=false` + `FF9SOUND_SONG_STOPCURRENT`, the `HonoluluFieldMain.cs:151` primitive); the `NetSyncDiorama` boot pre-swirl STOPS the field BGM instead of suspending (no slot to re-stack); the real-song lane keeps SUSPEND (two-machine proven — its field-reload restore is the intended resume). `NetSyncBattle.WireBenchBoot` gains `Boolean silent = false` (the silent bench ships `0xFFFE` instead of the 156 ending-theme bench via a derived `BenchSong`; the default keeps the 156 bench byte-for-byte) + `Ff9mkDebugMenu`’s "Bench (silent)" button to exercise the host-silent BGM lane SOLO. |
| `s55-netsync-unpair-guards.patch` | **THE UNPAIR / RECONNECT GUARDS (captured 2026-07-23; wire UNCHANGED at v10; 1 file: `NetSyncClient.cs`; ★ partially two-machine proven 2026-07-23).** Four independent robustness fixes for a following guest across a transport blip or battle-return: **(1)** the `[Netsync] AutoTeleport` knob (default OFF, pending a leash rework) gates the live-coop AUTO teleport-to-host arm — the selftest bench arm stays INDEPENDENT of the knob, and the manual F11/L3 teleport is a separate path, unaffected. **(2)** ENCOUNTER-SUPPRESSION HYSTERESIS — a ~1-3s relay blip no longer instantly re-enables the guest’s random encounters (which booted a LOCAL battle on the guest’s own party and crashed the battle-return seam): hold the pause while connected OR within a 5s `EncounterGraceMs` grace of the last connected frame, FOLLOWING-only, fail-safe after the grace (tick-baseline law, pre-expired). **(3)** `SnapToHostAuto` — the AUTO recovery captures the on-mesh position and RESTORES it when the host’s spot is off this walkmesh (the engine’s default-spawn dump can sit on a gateway quad and reload-cycle, and the player never asked to move); the human-initiated manual refloor still lands at the default spawn. **(4)** the exit-ramp SETTLE DEFERRAL — don’t fire `ExitMirrorToOwnSave`’s Autoload on the FIRST raw frame of a battle-return field load (it raced the return’s own load, the guest-crash seam): wait for `FieldHUD` control-return, bounded at 10s (`ExitRampSettleMaxMs`), the world map (gMode 3) exempt. |
| `s56-netsync-blip-grace.patch` | **THE BLIP-GRACE ROUND (captured 2026-07-23; wire UNCHANGED at v10; 4 files; ★ holding on the real link 2026-07-23).** Smooths a following guest through a relay transport blip (`IsConnected` false for the ~2.5-3s reconnect cycle) so a momentary drop doesn’t visibly tear down the session. `NetSyncClient.cs`: **(a)** GHOST BLIP-GRACE (`_ghostLastPairedTick` / `GhostGraceMs = 5000`, link-DOWN-gated) holds the peer’s ghost planted at its last pose through a transport drop instead of vanishing-and-popping-back — a deliberate leave (field-0 sentinel with the link UP) still despawns instantly; **(b)** the DIORAMA BLIP-HOLD (`_dioramaStaleSince` / `DioramaBlipHoldMs = 8000` + the `_dioramaWatcherLeftFinal` skip-nonce stamp) freezes a booted diorama while its lane is stale AND the transport is down, rather than the watcher’s shorter 2s horizon leaving and the same still-live nonce re-booting on reconnect ("battles start twice"), and stamps the skip on a REAL leave so the same fight can’t re-boot. Both transports gain `_breakReason` instrumentation (`NetSyncRelay.cs` + `NetSyncSocket.cs`, `ReadFull` static→instance) so the read loop stashes WHY a link ended — caught exception / orderly EOF / WS close code+reason / wire-version mismatch — logged ONCE at the "disconnected; retrying" line, never per read-iteration. And `Memoria.Prime/Log.cs:125` — the log-timestamp `hh`→`HH` (24-hour) fix: `hh` logged PM as its 12-hour hour with no AM/PM, so every afternoon line read 12h off. |
| `s57-netsync-transition-intent.patch` | **THE TRANSITION-INTENT LANE + DIALOGUE L1 (captured 2026-07-23; wire v10→**v11**; 5 files; ★ two-machine proven 2026-07-23).** Two new HOST→GUEST sections on the existing type-5 story lane make a following guest track the host's field changes and dialogue scenes without chasing the position lane. **(1) THE TRANSITION INTENT** (section 4, `[destField u16 LE][nonce u8]`): the host announces its field change at the MAPJUMP commit — the `return 4` funnel is the only decidably field→field point (WMAPJUMP shares `SetNextMap` but is world-map exit, excluded) — via `EmitFieldIntentIfHost`, so a FOLLOWING guest warps IN PARALLEL instead of debounce-chasing the position lane; the serial `FollowHostTick` stays the guaranteed floor if the intent is lost. **(2) DIALOGUE L1 CO-LOCATION** (section 5, `[flag u8][nonce u8]`): flag 1 = the host is in a blocking dialogue event → a co-located guest snaps to the host once (`SnapToHost`) and HOLDS control so both watch the scene from the same start point; releases on flag-clear / leaving the field / a follow-warp takeover / link drop. Wire **v10→v11** (`NetSyncSocket.cs` + the wire-format doc): the sections ride the type-5 lane and the section-walk skips unknown ids, so a new section is not a wire break by itself — the Version bump only keeps mixed engine builds from half-syncing (v10-and-older peers version-rejected). `NetSyncState.cs`: the `SectionIntent=4`/`SectionEvent=5` consts + pure `AppendSection`/`TryReadSection` helpers (allocation-only, bounds-checked, never throw — the SAME encoder/decoder the state builder and the offline `SelfTest` share; the SelfTest now round-trips both sections, proves an unknown id declines, and re-parses the story past them). `NetSyncClient.cs` (the bulk): the F2 statics; `StartFollowWarp` extracted verbatim from `FollowHostTick` so the serial follow AND the parallel intent fast-path share ONE fade+warp body (stamps `_followWarpedTo` → the serial same-dest guard suppresses a redundant warp); `EmitFieldIntentIfHost` (host+socket only, ENDING excluded, an out-of-band frame push so the intent leaves at the transition instant, not the next 7 Hz tick); `BuildStateFrame` (the ONE host frame builder for both the ~7 Hz publish and the intent emit — a null story ships nothing, as the bare `SnapshotAll` publish did); `UpdateHostEventFlag` (`!GetUserControl()` corroborated with a live `UIManager.Dialogs.Visible` so a gateway MOVQ can't false-trip, edge-logged only); `ServiceIntentFollow` (guest fast-path ahead of the serial debounce, nonce-dedup, DEFER-not-mark mid-menu/diorama, DECLINE-and-mark if the field isn't on this install); `ServiceEventCoLocate` + `ReleaseL1` (incl. the post-review pin-flag coupling fix — `_l1Pinned` re-coupled in the hold block + the `|| _l1WeForcedControl` release gate, so a mid-pin blip can't strand control OFF); `DebugInjectIntent`/`DebugInjectIntentImpl` + `DebugHostEventFlag`; the `Update` wiring (publish → `BuildStateFrame`, the two `Service*` calls, the host intent-clear + event-flag block, the selftest L1 bench); `ResetIntentAndL1State` on `ApplyConfigChange`/`OnDestroy`. `EventEngine.DoEventCode.cs`: the one `EmitFieldIntentIfHost` line at the MAPJUMP commit, after the F1 `RedirectGuestFieldJump`. `Ff9mkDebugMenu.cs`: the debug FIELD-warp (`ServicePendingWarp`) intent emit (the overworld `WorldWarp` mode-3 deliberately NOT hooked — world-map is frontier) + the Transition-intent + Dialogue-L1 selftest bench sections in the field-gate bench. Vanilla + a non-following guest untouched; identity-safe. | `s97-netsync-diorama-warm-return.patch` | **F3.3 DIO-1 — THE DIORAMA WARM RETURN: a free-standing guest comes back from a battle diorama IN PLACE, through Main_Reinit, the way a stock random battle returns (built (compile-checked), DEPLOYED LIVE 2026-10-12 with s98+s99 (`build_memoria.py --label pre-s97-s99-coop-f33`, live `3b4d4c4474b049cd…`, both arches sha-verified, backup `20261012-184529`); ★ SOLO-PROVEN `coop_diorama_return.py` 22/22 twice (warm return moved 0.0 vs a Main_Init placement 649 away; the same position test reads NOT KEPT on the cold leg, 1380 away); ★★ TWO-MACHINE PROVEN 2026-10-12 (save moogle key relay at Dali 351, warm diorama return on field 250); NOT harness-, in-game- or two-machine-proven; wire UNCHANGED at v13; harness protocol stays **5** (additive); 5 files, 22 hunks, +640/−11, no new `.cs`, no csproj change).** WHY ([`../studies/field-coop/F3.2-DESIGN.md`](../studies/field-coop/F3.2-DESIGN.md) DIO-1; F1-BUILD.md calls it "the next warp"): a stock battle saves the field in two halves (`BackupPosObjData` on the field side, then the battle's own `StartEvents` copies `sEventContext0` into `sEventContext1`); the diorama did neither — `isDebug` skips the battle `StartEvents` and every field load zeroes `sEventContext1.inited` — so EVERY return cold-started through Main_Init (MAP vars zeroed, the guest moved to the arrival point, NPCs reset) while the host came back in place. `NetSyncDiorama.cs` (the bulk): `PrimeWarmReturn`, after every boot refusal and right before the Replace, runs the pure `WarmVerdict` and, only when FREE-STANDING, does the stock field half itself (`BackupPosObjData`, then `sEventContext1.copy(sEventContext0)`). Free-standing = field mode, a started context for THIS field, the engine's own slot empty, an entry-0 tag-10 Main_Reinit (432 of 817 stock scripts have none, and a reinit there leaves every object suspended), random encounters armed (`encratio > 0`; the other 209 Main_Reinits continue one scripted fight), entry 0 above level 0 and no battle-return bracket open (`BattleReturnInFlight`), control on, FieldHUD, no dialogue window of any kind, a controlled actor; anything else keeps today's cold return and logs one reason line. THE STALE-COPY LAW: `UndoWarm` (slot `inited = 0`; on a boot field still alive, MovePC un-frozen and the boot's suspended audio resumed) runs on Boot's catch, a Replace that never started, `ForceDisarm` on a live boot field, a Leave toward another field, a superseding boot, and any field load that is not this diorama's own return (`BeforeFieldEvents`, first call in `NetSyncClient.ApplyStoryBeforeEvents`, one Boolean read otherwise). Music: Main_Reinit replays none in 366 of 385 stock fields, so a warm boot SUSPENDS the field song on every lane (host-silent included; unknown/manual through `HonoluluFieldMain`'s stock block verbatim) and the warm return stops the battle song over it; cold boots keep the s54 STOP and the B3.1 skip. `EventEngine.cs`: two one-line hooks in `StartEvents`, one inside each branch, report the branch the engine took (`NoteFieldEventsStart`, with `Request`'s own result kept): `warm`, `cold`, or `reinit-missing` (`Log.Error`, never "warm"). `NetSyncClient.cs`: the exit ramp's settle also waits for `!BattleReturnInFlight` (a warm copy restores usercontrol inside `StartEvents`). `NetSyncParty.cs` (DIO-6, partial): `BenchMirrorArmed` lets the teardown clear a bench-armed mirror even if the role dropped mid-diorama. `SelfTest` scripts the warm-gate truth table (detail ends `warm[gate=ok return=ok]`). `HarnessAgent.cs`: the `netsync.diorama` block (`booted`/`boot`/`boot_why`/`boot_field`/`boot_x`/`boot_z`/`primed`/`returns`/`last_return`/`last_return_field`/`resuming`; sentinels inside the catch line's second literal), verbs `netsync dioboot <scene> [pattern]` (selftest role, live field only) and `dioleave`, and the release now Leaves a harness-booted diorama and holds the selftest role until it tears down (`ServiceHarnessDiorama`, 1 s retry). Vanilla inert: every new engine-side call is a static no-op unless a diorama boot armed it. Known gaps: Main_Reinit reads `GetBattleResult` 0 where the host reads its real result (9 of 385 stock Main_Reinit read it, all as a defeat test); a warm return keeps the boot-time `partyUID`. Guest `Observe`/`Merge`: parsed once per new payload, take events where `(Int16)(seq − lastSeen) > 0`; a session's first merge only baselines; a gap logs once and those lines fall to the orphan rule; KEY (kind 2) is counted and ignored (R5 not built). A new epoch (Guid-drawn, never 0 or either of the last two) or a same-epoch head that went BACKWARDS clears the inbox, re-baselines at the head and resets the dedup nonces, so no history replays. Apply (R4): a closes-bit event re-applies page by page until the guest's window closes; a non-closing advance on the last page is a no-op; the relay-accept purge uses each event's own scene nonce; the host's open-set cursor drives the cursor lock (skipped on a voiced-choice commit); the open set claims a window against orphaning. R6: a heartbeat frozen 10 s drops the session half of the lock (one line), and windows open when it moves again stay local; R7: chips for a host in a menu or a battle; R8: the relay accept parses party sections on a mirroring guest. `NetSyncClient.cs`: the host tap appends to the ring instead of `SendDialog`, `UpdateHostScene` scans the open set and pushes when dirty, every host frame carries section 7 (flags b0 scene, b1 control, b2 input line open, b3 on field, b4 non-HUD UI, b5 battle); the guest reads ONE payload per tick (closes a host-relaunch replay race); co-located also needs `!b2`, and off-field = `!b3` or the C2 `Field == 0`, never in battle. `NetSyncSocket.cs`: v14, type 7 retired (kept compiled). `NetSyncState.cs`: `SectionDialog = 7`. `HarnessAgent.cs`: verb `netsync host open|shut|adv|choice|key|gap|epoch|freeze|menu|battle` (selftest role, bench + lock on; a bench host model builds section 7 through the real builder each tick), the C2 `advance`/`choice`/`unmatched` verbs and the ~ menu inject now go through the ring, and flat additive keys `wire`/`epoch`/`head_seq`/`last_seen`/`gaps`/`heartbeat`/`host_stalled`/`host_flags`/`host_open[]`/`cursor`/`key_ignored`/`host_frozen`/`host_menu`/`host_battle` (sentinels inside the catch line's second literal). `SelfTest` gains `RingScript` (round trip, every truncated prefix refused, wrap 65535→1, a gap >32 once, a re-send applied once, KEY ignored, epoch reset without replay incl. a rising session and a same-epoch head regression, stall across the tick wrap). Not built: R5 (the KEY relay) and the R9 `tap-record`/`replay` bench, so the host-emit side (closes bit, page byte, open-set scan, flags, heartbeat) is only RingScript-checked and bench-fed with hand-built bytes until two-machine. | `s100-netsync-host-command-phase-gates.patch` | **B4/B5 STAGE 1 — HOST COMMAND-PHASE GATES: the host's Ready bit, its command intake and its type-4 roster open only in the command phase (built (compile-checked), DEPLOYED LIVE 2026-10-13 with s100-s105 (`build_memoria.py --label pre-s100-s105-battle-b45`, backup `20261013-042524`); ★ SOLO-PROVEN coop_battle_host_gates.py 20/20, calibrated red on s99; regression set unchanged (diorama 22/22, talk relay 28/28, dialogue authority 147/147); two-machine pending; NOT harness-, in-game- or two-machine-proven; host-only — the guest only stores the new caps byte, which it does not read yet; wire UNCHANGED at v14 (type-1 header flags bit 1 is additive: an older peer's `ParseView`/`WireBenchBoot` read bit 0 only); harness protocol stays **5** (additive); 3 files, 26 hunks, +583/−11, no new `.cs`, no csproj change).** WHY ([`../studies/battle-coop/B4-B5-DESIGN.md`](../studies/battle-coop/B4-B5-DESIGN.md) §6.1, stage 1): the host advertised a unit as Ready from the HUD's stale `ReadyQueue`/`InputFinishList` and took a type-2 command in any phase, so a frame left over from the victory edge or the previous battle drained into the next intro (THE FREEZE: the opening camera never hands over, the phase sticks below 4) or raced `KillNormalCommand` at the victory edge, and the roster's 1 s cadence could delay the first turn. `NetSyncBattle.cs` (the bulk): one never-throwing predicate `HostCommandPhaseOpen` = `btl_phase == PHASE_NORMAL && FF9BMenu_IsEnable()` gates (a) the per-unit Ready bit in `SampleOwnBattle`, (b) intake in `HandleCommandPayload`, right after the length/version check and BEFORE `IsGuestOwnedSlot` (each refusal counts in `cmd_refused`; one log line per battle per reason: intro / scripted event / menu-off / victory / defeat / closing / menu disabled), and (c) `BuildRoster` (null outside the phase; the latest-slot lane keeps the last roster, so a pause does not blink it). Type-1 header flags bit 1 `CapCommandPhaseGated` sits beside bit 0, and `ParseView` keeps the whole byte as `BattleView.HostCaps`. `TrackCommandPhase` (every tick, in `Pump` and `SelfTestPump`) samples type-1 at once on a phase edge and, on the opening edge, retries the roster for up to 8 ticks until one ships. Every former silent return in `HandleCommandPayload` now logs and counts in `cmd_dropped`; the three existing refusal lines keep their text. Accepted guest-visible effect (§6.1): a host pause, the in-battle menu or Libra drops every Ready bit, so the guest's digit pick resets to the root and its `GuestWaitMs` episode restarts instead of burning. Harness (selftest only): `netsync.battle` = `host{gates_off, guest_slots, cmd_phase, caps, ready_mask, cmd_refused, cmd_dropped, cmd_queued, refused_why, dropped_why, roster_lag, loop_cmds}`, `peer{live, seq, nonce, caps, ready_mask}`, `menu{roster_slots}` (sentinels inside the catch line's second literal); verbs `netsync guestslots <mask>` (a process-local override of the `GuestSlots = 0` pin, battle lane only), `netsync bcmd <hex>` (a raw type-2 frame on the loopback, drained through the real `HandleCommandPayload` inside an own battle) and `netsync hostgates 1\|0` (the calibration lever: `0` reverts exactly this stage's paths; it needs `IsSelfTestRole`, so it can never ungate a real host). `NetSyncClient.cs`: `HarnessGuestSlots`, and every harness release (disarm, fault, `reset`, `selftest 0`; forced role or ini selftest alike) runs `NetSyncBattle.HarnessReleaseBench` (lever back on, parked `bcmd` frames discarded) and restores the pin. Vanilla inert: `Pump`/`SelfTestPump` return at once with netsync disabled; the host's own battle changes only through the gates, plus one extra type-1 sample per phase edge. Gates: `emit --files <the 3>` onto the stack through s99 (the baseline is byte-equal to the pre-edit backups `*.pre-b4-stage1.20261012-203700`): 54,241 bytes, 3 files, 0 no-newline markers, CRLF kept (`HarnessAgent.cs` LF), no BOM or non-ASCII added. | `s101-netsync-guest-containment-backstop.patch` | **B4/B5 STAGE 2 — THE GUEST CONTAINMENT BACKSTOP: inside a battle diorama a player's `cmd[0]`/`cmd[3]` is never enqueued locally (R9), and no slot reads guest-owned (G2) (built (compile-checked), DEPLOYED LIVE 2026-10-13 with s100-s105 (`build_memoria.py --label pre-s100-s105-battle-b45`, backup `20261013-042524`); ★ SOLO-PROVEN coop_battle_containment.py 23/23, calibrated red on s99; regression set unchanged (diorama 22/22, talk relay 28/28, dialogue authority 147/147); two-machine pending; NOT harness-, in-game- or two-machine-proven; guest-side, keyed on `NetSyncDiorama.Active` alone, inert in production until stage 3 fills a diorama `ReadyQueue`; wire UNCHANGED at v14 (no type, layout or header bit); harness protocol stays **5** (additive); 4 files, 16 hunks, +353/−7, no new `.cs`, no csproj change; `btl_cmd.cs` joins the stack).** WHY ([`../studies/battle-coop/B4-B5-DESIGN.md`](../studies/battle-coop/B4-B5-DESIGN.md) §4.3 + §3.8 item 1, stage 2): B4 lets the stock command menu open in a diorama, so every commit site gets a capture (stage 3); this is the layer behind them — a missed site becomes one logged refusal instead of a re-simulation of the host's fight (`CommandEngine` runs ungated there and spends the guest's REAL bag). `btl_cmd.cs`: `SetCommand`'s first statement is `if (NetSyncDiorama.RefuseLocalEnqueue(cmd, commandId, sub_no, tar_id)) return;`, ahead of `CheckUsingCommand` and every write to the caster — two Boolean reads and false outside a booted diorama. `NetSyncDiorama.cs`: the pure `R9Slot` returns 0/3 only when `Active`, the lever is on, the owner is a player and the command IS its `cmd[0]`/`cmd[3]` by reference; everything else (`cmd[1]`/`[2]`/`[4]`/`[5]`, enemies, a no-slot command such as `cmd_escape`, null, an exception) is −1 = stock. That reaches every `BattleHUD` Send* site, auto-battle, Vivi auto-magic and (intended, correction 1) Berserk/Confuse, SysPhantom and `SummonEiko`. NOT reached: the debug `BattleUI` (`playCommand` enqueues a fresh `CMD_DATA`, `SetDebugEnemyCommand` is an enemy `EnqueueCommand`) — held out ONLY by `BattleUI.OnGUI`'s `Active` early-return, their sole caller; R9 must not widen to every player regist (it would catch `cmd_escape`, stage 12's). DEVIATIONS (owner's eye): the bench feed writes the PRODUCTION peer view from inside a selftest diorama (so the mirror, the digit panel and `netsync.battle.peer` read the exact production path; it ticks only while Active and stops with its diorama, a new boot, `bbench stop` or harness release); `CloseAll` only when the mirror holds slots, not on every failed frame; pending is recorded only while serving (a stage-3 bench capture behaves as before); open and close through stage 3's helpers, not raw `AddPlayerToReady`/`RemovePlayerFromAction(1<<L)`; the mirror reads `UnitView.Ready` straight from the view (stage 3's `PeerUnit.Ready` stays, unused); accepted and obsolete are counted apart; a Ready slot the roster does not carry waits (`mirror.no_roster`, logged once), bounded by the fixer; the extra bench levers (`knob`, `freeze`, `stop`, `unmap none`); and an owner-visible effect worth a sitting-1 check — while the guest's own diorama is still in its intro, the host's Ready bit opens neither surface (before stage 8 the digit panel served there). HOST (`NetSyncBattle.cs`): `NoteOwnBattleStart` latches the stock expression (`cfg.skip_btl_camera == 0 && isRandomEncounter`, `HonoluluBattleMain.cs:167`) and logs it once; `SampleOwnBattle` sets `CapIntroMirror` 0x08 (+ `FlagIntroSkipped` 0x04) beside bit 1, so own-battle caps read 0x0A/0x0E (0x0B/0x0F random); `WireIntro(f)` decodes (-1 = bit 3 clear); `TryGetPeerBattleOpen` gains `out hostFlags`. GUEST (`NetSyncDiorama.cs`, `NetSyncClient.cs`): `_wireIntro` follows `_wireSongId` (stamped by `BootFromWire`'s trailing `wireIntro = -1`, never onto a live boot; reset by the manual `Boot`, Boot's catch and `ForceDisarm`); ONE seam `SkipBattleIntro(stock)` wraps the two stock sites (`HonoluluBattleMain.Start`'s `SFX.SkipCameraAnimation`, `battle.BattleLoadLoop`'s `noDelayFade`) and is one Boolean read unless a diorama is booted; `LiveTick` (transport branch after `ReconcileTick`, selftest after `StockMenuTick` — review ctrl F1) latches `_normalMs`; the BOOT line gains `intro=` and `swirl=`. HARNESS (`HarnessAgent.cs`, LF kept; `Ff9mkDebugMenu.HarnessBattle` gains an optional `rand`): `battle <scene> [group] [rand]`, `netsync dioboot <scene> [pattern] [wire] [skip\|play\|own] [rand]`, `bench intro 1\|0` (the lever `BenchIntroOff`, both halves, selftest only), `bench cfg skipcam 0\|1\|restore`; keys `netsync.battle.host.cfg`/`.intro` and `netsync.diorama.intro`, with sentinels in the `AppendNetsync` catch literal. | `s119-netsync-diorama-hold-on-peer.patch` | **R3 SITTING-A FIX #2 — THE DIORAMA HOLD KEYED ON THE PEER: on the GUEST a stale battle lane means the host's fight is OVER only when the peer is alive (PEER-ALIVE = `IsConnected && GetRemote().Valid`, never `IsConnected` alone) without a break for the settle AND says so (back on a real field, or this boot's end block read terminal while the lane was live); every other stale lane HOLDS the booted diorama frozen on the same nonce until the lane resumes or the cap (built (compile-checked), DEPLOYED LIVE 2026-10-14 with s118-s121 (`build_memoria.py`, backup `20261014-230936`; Assembly-CSharp `685e76902524e207`); ★ SOLO-PROVEN coop_cut_hold.py legs s119 12/12 (a 6 s cut held 4047 ms on nonce 1 and resumed, the ready mirror SERVING again) and span 9/9 (the unseen victory left why host_field after a 12 s hold); s117 reproduced the sitting-A LEAVE + watcher-leave pair in both; calibrated red on s117 (R3-SITTING-A-FIXES.md phase 1); two-machine pending (R3 sitting B); wire UNCHANGED at v14; harness protocol stays **5** (additive); 3 files, 13 hunks, +525/−31, no new `.cs`, no csproj change).** WHY ([`../studies/battle-coop/R3-SITTING-A-FIXES.md`](../studies/battle-coop/R3-SITTING-A-FIXES.md) s119): the s56 watcher read `_socket.IsConnected` (stale + link up = over), and behind the local bridge the hop stays up through any cut under its 45 s redial cap — sitting A's 6 s cut left within ~2 s and stamped the skip; the s56 hold never ran. GUEST (`NetSyncClient.cs`): the booted branch is one pure step, `DioramaWatchStep` → NOTHING / HOLD / LEAVE_FINAL (why `new_nonce` / `terminal` / `host_field` / `cap`); `LatchDioramaTerminal` records `NetSyncEnd.IsTerminal` while the lane is live (TryGetPeerEnd serves only the live view). THE SETTLE (`DioramaSettleMs` 500; review S119-R1/R2): a cut's battle lane goes stale up to one SampleMs (150) before GetRemote does, so an over-verdict needs the peer alive without a break for 500 ms of the stale stretch (which also closes the resume race), and a hold is counted and logged only at 500 ms, naming the settled peer state — "peer silent mid-battle -- holding ..." (a cut) or "...battle lane stalled mid-battle with the peer alive on field F" (a host stall); the cost is a genuine end's watcher leave 0.5 s later (the B5 end-leave still leads). A decided LEAVE_FINAL stands until the lane is live on the booted fight again, so a deferred leave never turns back into a hold. The cap stays 8 s behind the one seam `DioramaHoldCapMs` (s120 replaces its body); a leave past it is today's final leave (skip stamped). Hold/resume lines capped at 3 pairs per boot; the selftest proof block gains "diorama watcher selftest: OK (24 rows)" (`DioramaWatchSelfTest`: the sitting-A row, the settle rows, four sequences). STOCK MENU (`NetSyncBattle.StockMenu.cs`): `NoteDioramaResume` re-arms the ready mirror's once-per-boot SERVING and no-lane lines after a logged resume (the `view == null` literal stays as it was: an offline stack pin reads it). HARNESS (`HarnessAgent.cs`, LF kept): `netsync.diorama.hold` {`state`, `since_ms`, `cap_ms`, `settle_ms`, `holds`, `resumes`, `gaveup`, `last_ms`, `peer_alive`, `host_field`, `terminal`, `why`}, sentinels in the `AppendNetsync` catch literal; no new verb (the watcher needs a real transport, so its bench is the T2 wire replay). OLD PEERS: the step reads only the host's existing lanes, so any host works; a pre-B5 host (no end block) whose position never reads a real field holds to the cap, then leaves as today; an s118-or-older guest keeps the s56 predicate; vanilla, solo and hosts never reach it (the booted branch needs a following guest's wire boot). NOT HERE (s120): the 3 s exit ramp, OvlConnecting and the ghost blip-grace still key on IsConnected. CONFIRMED on s121 by moving T2 cuts 70 ms (run dirs `20261015-074620-confirm-s119`, `-074825-confirm-s120-battle`): s119 at `--blackhole-at` 81.66 (tail 0 → ~67) read outage 4032 / hold 2094, offset 1938 (predicted 1898-1983; S119-G3 red); s120-battle at 79.66 (tail 66 → 0) read 18015 / 18047, offset −32 (predicted −50..+35, green). HARNESS (`HarnessAgent.cs`, LF kept): `netsync.battle.peer.expired` {`seq`, `nonce` (the memo; -1 = none), `count` (run-outs), `refused` (episodes), `frames` (refused repeat frames) — the three counters monotonic, read DELTAS — `ms` (the last episode's run-out to its last refused repeat: the tail less up to two Updates; -1 = none)}, the last key of `peer`, sentinels in the `AppendNetsync` catch literal (its SECOND literal); no new verb (the run-out needs a real transport: the bench is the T2 replay with a blackhole). A tail ≥ 66 cut reads `count` +1, `refused` +1, `ms` ≈ tail; a tail-0 cut `count` +1, `refused` +0; the key absent = an engine with the defect. OLD PEERS: guest-local, reading only the host's existing type-1 lane, so any host works; an s121-or-older guest keeps the re-adopt; vanilla, solo and a host with no guest never receive a peer battle frame, so the memo never arms; the selftest role's loopback and the bench feed write `_peerView` directly (no run-out, no memo). THE SIDE EFFECT (owner decision: KEPT): a host whose game THREAD stalls while its writer keeps re-sending one seq (the writer re-adds the sticky `_outBattle` every 33 ms, so the guest's transport serves the frozen frame for the whole stall) was masked by the re-adopt at ANY length and every time (a stale blink and a menu close every ~2 s, no hold, the s120 stall cap unreachable); now the lane goes stale ~2 s in and STAYS stale: past ~2.5 s (the run-out + the 500 ms settle) it is a counted 'stalled' hold (the stock menu shuts) that resumes on the same nonce at the host's first new seq, and at ~10 s (the run-out + the 8 s `DioramaStallCapMs`) the diorama LEAVES FINAL with the skip stamped (review S131-CONS-2: the ~79% figure is for finite tails — cuts and genuine ends — only). ## Performance / quality — shipped in the bundle, not a fidelity patch

| File | What it fixes |
|---|---|
| `s35-overlay-texture-cache.patch` | **RETIRED 2026-07-29 (owner call) — reverted from the live tree; the file stays for provenance.** Was: an overlay-PNG **decode cache** for pure-`.bgx` scenes (`static Dictionary<String, Texture2D> MemoriaOverlayTextureCache` in `BGSCENE_DEF.ProcessMemoriaOverlay`'s `Image` op, keyed by resolved path). Why retired: (1) the cache key had NO file identity, so a re-deployed overlay PNG at the same path served the STALE decode for the whole session — the Trace-tab cut-out loop's "art needs a full relaunch" playtest bug (2026-07-29); (2) the 2026-07-07 ★ "fade fixed" claim was CONFOUNDED — the field-entry/entry-settle work churned concurrently and the benefit was never isolated (owner: "never confirmed to be useful"). Removal restores hot-reloadable field art (~ → Reload picks up re-deployed PNGs). If the see-through fade provably returns, reinstate WITH path+mtime+size invalidation (s22's `Reload + anims` precedent for the same disease class). Reverse-applied `-F0` clean; no sibling patch carries its lines as context (s12/s32/s33/s65 checked); DWIXNoDeploy compile-check clean with the game up, then the deploying build closed-game 2026-07-29: Output == both arches sha `44090B29…`, cache symbol verified ABSENT + the fork-gate symbols verified present, pre-build full-set DLL backup `preS35removal.20260729-114832`. ★ **Owner-confirmed same day: re-deployed field art hot-reloads via ~ → Reload ("image movement is hot-reloadable now"); no fade regression reported.** |
| `s87-field70-movepc-walkmesh-guard.patch` | **A STOCK MEMORIA BUG: the field-70 `MovePC` NullReferenceException storm (★ BUILT + DEPLOYED + HARNESS-PROVEN 2026-09-23, `build_memoria.py --label s87-field70-walkmesh`, 0 errors, Output == both arches sha `1b16d54f95add005…`; pre-build backup `20260923-133716`).** ★ `studies/test-harness/field70_movepc.py` 5/5 in-game (run `20260923-133908-s87-field70`): **0 MovePC NREs over the whole 123.5 s field-70 stay** (was 3676), field 70 still hands off by its own `Field(4600)` at the same moment, the player walks 575u on 4600, and ZERO exceptions in either log for the whole run. One file, `FieldMapActorController.cs`, one condition. `FieldMap.LoadFieldMap` returns early for field 70 ("Opening-For FMV", `FieldMap.cs:435`) before it builds a walkmesh. But `MovePC`'s `PSXMovementMethod` slope multiply (`:740-741`, upstream `1bc87856`, still unguarded on origin/main) reads `this.fieldMap.walkMesh` on every tick, moving or not. The script's placeholder Zidane (`DefinePlayerCharacter`, before the movie; `DisableMove()` only after it) therefore threw once per logic tick. Measured by the harness with no input: **3676 NREs over the 123 s opening (29.8/s)** into `output_log.txt`, zero on every other field. That is the ~26-30 "per bench run" every harness run showed — New Game→warp dwell, not the bench. On stock Memoria a human New Game hits it too. Each throw aborted `HonoBehaviorSystem.Update` after the controller (no per-behaviour catch), but the `.eb` interpreter and MBG run earlier in the list, so the movie played and the `Field()` warp fired: no observed gameplay effect. Fix: `&& this.fieldMap.walkMesh != null` — identical on every field that has a walkmesh. Gates: forward `git apply --check` + `patch -F0` onto the `--stop-after s85` baseline, reverse against the clone, replay with s87 installed `==` live. |

| `s49-world-smoother-anim-guard.patch` | **The RegisterState anim-null guard (2026-07-22, found via the custom-vehicle rung-1 bench).** `SmoothFrameUpdater_World.RegisterState` resolved every world actor's current anim unguarded: an actor whose `originalActor.anim` id is missing from AnimationDB — or whose Animation component holds no clip under that name — made `anim[curAnim]` return null and the `.time` reads NRE **every frame**, inside `HonoBehaviorSystem.Update`'s sweep — which aborts the sweep and with it the WORLD SCRIPT TICK (the HonoBehavior teardown law's exception shape): the player still walks (engine-side) while every `.eb` per-frame loop is silently dead.
