One file: `SmoothFrameUpdater_World.cs`. |
| `s78-debug-menu-flag-band-labels.patch` | **THE ~ MENU'S FLAG-BAND LABELS, CORRECTED (★ IN-GAME CONFIRMED 2026-08-02 — owner playtest after relaunch, `~` → Flags tab; one file, `Ff9mkDebugMenu.cs`; found in the completion-journal T0 scoping pass).** Three wrong numbers in the Flags tab, all one class — the readout mirrored `ff9mapkit/ff9mapkit/flags.py` as it stood in mid-2026 and was never re-mirrored after the band corrections landed there. **(1) `StorySummary`'s "chests opened" was a meaningless total**: it popcounted `gEventGlobal` bytes 1047–1063 = bits **8376–8511**, which is the MOGNET give/read variant lock table (`flags.MOGNET_LOCK_LO/HI`), not chests — real FF9 keeps **no per-chest registry anywhere**. Replaced with the metric the engine actually scores: `EventState.GetTreasureHunterPoints()` (already on that line) **plus its rank H..S**, thresholds verbatim from that method's own comment block (`EventState.cs:53-71` — bytes 896–960 + 966–975 at 1 pt/bit, 182–186 at 2 pts/bit). `CountBits` was the popcount's only caller and is deleted with it. **(2) `RegionLabel` still called 8376–8511 `chest_opened`** — same mislabel, now split into the three real sub-bands (give locks 8376–8439 / read locks 8440–8503 / margin 8504–8511) plus the stock mognet mailbox 8192–8367 it never covered at all. **(3) ⚠ THE LIVE SAVE-CORRUPTER: `RegionLabel` told an author `[safe custom band]` from bit 8512**, but `flags.FIRST_SAFE_FLAG` is **8712** — bits 8512–8711 are stock read-mail payload, whole-byte-written by ordinary Mognet play. It also claimed safety all the way to 16320, over the kit's nameplate-explored words / QTE scratch / netsync coop cells. Corrected to **[8712, 16048)**, with those four bands above it labelled; the comment now says outright that flags.py is the authority and a green label is a hint, not a clearance. **NOT changed — a reported finding that did not hold:** `bit == 184 \|\| bit == 191` is **correct**, not a bit-index-vs-byte-offset confusion. Those are byte 23's engine handshake bits (`flags.py` `field_menu_guard` / `boot_scratch`; `research/STORY_FLAGS.md` row **23**), and the chocograph/dig **BYTES** 184–191 are a *different location* (bits 1472–1535) — now labelled separately, with the aliasing spelled out in-comment so the next reader does not re-file it. Behavior outside the Flags tab is untouched. Gate: the patch applied to the pre-edit snapshot reproduces the live file **byte-for-byte** (`d151d27a…`). Verify in-game: `~` → Flags tab — read the summary line, then type a bit index (8600 → read-mail payload; 8712 → safe; 184 → field menu guard; 1480 → chocograph). |

| `s76-sfx-state-payload.patch` | **THE DR_MOVE / DR_TPAGE PAYLOAD (★ BUILT + DEPLOYED 2026-07-30; a LOG-FIELD round — additive only, zero behavior change, no new ini key). One file (`Memoria/Battle/SFX/SfxMeshProbe.cs`), built on the s53 tip; 3 hunks, +67/−6; 0 errors / 186 warnings, both arches sha `b6ce810fa9b710b8…` == `Output\Assembly-CSharp.dll`; all 8 new literals + the retained s47/s48/s53/s58 markers verified in metadata (UTF-16 `#US` for literals, UTF-8 `#Strings` for symbols); pre-build DLL backup `20260730-222511` → `py tools/restore_memoria_dll.py 20260730-222511`.** WHY: the U1 second-array cast came back VISIBLE_UNBANDED (column-640 geometry drew, neither marked texel cell sampled) and its probe log shows **641 `DR_MOVE` packets on ef038 in two blocks (effect frames 86–138, 237–380) where the static direction-law census records ZERO** — the leading mechanism is a runtime VRAM blit wiping the marks, but the log recorded DR_MOVE **without its rects** and DR_TPAGE **without the code word** (41,561 bare rows), so it could neither be confirmed nor tied to a primitive. Now `LogPrim()` dispatches per-type on the raw code, mirroring `SFXRender`'s own decode field-for-field: **`DR_TPAGE` (225)** appends `tpage,tx,ty,abr,tp,len` — `tpage` = low16 of `code[0]` exactly as `SFXRender.cs:430` feeds `SFXKey.SetCurrentTPage`, bit fields per `PSXGPU.cs:25-43` (`tx=&15` ×64px, `ty=(>>4)&1` ×256px, `abr=(>>5)&3`, `tp=(>>7)&3`), so the marked page at VRAM (640,256) is simply `tx=10,ty=1` — this also settles the U1 study's open twin-pair co-draw question, which is why it rides the same edit; **`DR_MOVE` (231)** appends `rx,ry,rw,rh,x,y,path,len` — SOURCE rect origin+size and DEST origin exactly as `SFXRender.cs:455-462` decodes them (`ry` signed), with `path` recording the engine's own branch at `:464-472` (**`MV`** = `SFXMoveImage`→`PSXTextureMgr.MoveImage`, the real VRAM blit; **`SS`** = `SFXScreenShot`, a framebuffer grab, taken when `rx<320 && ry<240`) — so "did something blit over (640,256) before the scored surfaces drew" becomes a one-grep answer instead of an offline re-derivation. Both log `tag->getLen()` because `PSXGPU.cs:607-609` calls code 225 with `len==2` **DR_MODE** while `SFXRender.cs:302-309` routes every 225 to `DR_TPAGE()` regardless — a `len!=1` row must not be read as a page select. SAFETY: reads are per-type and never generic — DR_TPAGE's word is inside the 8 bytes `LogPrim` already dereferences (P_TAG's `r0/g0/b0/code` word **is** `DR_TPAGE.code[0]`), and DR_MOVE's `code[1..3]` are dereferenced by `SFXRender.DR_MOVE()` on the same pointer two statements after `LogPrim` returns, so the probe cannot fault where the frame would not; body stays inside the existing `try{}catch{}`. Row **COUNT** is unchanged (fields APPENDED to the existing `STATE` row), so the `PrimCap` contract and its truncation marker are byte-identical; measured cost ≈ **+2.9 %** on a 23 MB cast log (+0.65 MB DR_TPAGE, +17 KB DR_MOVE). Rides the existing `[SfxProbe] CapturePrims` gate — **`Memoria.ini` NOT touched** (already armed), `PrimSummary=1` still returns early, off by default for a normal install. ⚠ **CONSUMER HAZARD — `STATE` rows are now RAGGED by label.** All 3 committed STATE parsers (`thomas-swap/m0/{camera_match,verify_camera_match/indep_verify,verify_depth/scan_structure}.py`) are positional through `p[2]`/`p[5]` with `IndexError` guards and are unaffected; every other committed reader filters to PRIM/MODEL/ROOT/MESH/BONES — **0 committed parsers break** (verified by grep, incl. zero uses of the `endswith(label)` idiom). The one break is the SCRATCH throwaway `castread/adversarial/scripts/a22_drawproof.py:37` (`line.rstrip().endswith('DR_MOVE')`) — the very script that produced the 641-packet finding. It fails **SILENTLY to zero**, the dangerous direction (a zero census would read as "mechanism refuted"); fix is one line: `',231,DR_MOVE' in line`. Header now documents the ragged shape and says *parse positionally, never with `endswith(label)`*. BUILD-RECORD NOTE: the patch was cut from a **before/after snapshot pair** in scratch, not `git diff` in the shared clone — `SfxMeshProbe.cs` is a staged `A` there and the tree carries 73 files of concurrent-session edits, so a clone-side diff would have emitted the whole file plus foreign hunks; reverse-checked with `git apply` onto the before-snapshot, reproducing the live file **byte-for-byte** (`35602c6c…`). Provenance: engine code only, reads the packet header the engine itself decodes on the same frame; no asset bytes, no geometry, no SE data — same class as the s48 output capture. Interpretation caveat: a block of `SS` rather than `MV` packets would **refute** the leading mechanism, not confirm it. |
| `s77-sfx-uv-range.patch` | **THE UVR ROW — per-mesh UV ranges (★ BUILT + DEPLOYED 2026-07-31, owner-authorized after a classifier stop: the patch was cut and reverse-checked byte-identical onto the s76-tip snapshot `35602c6c…` → `6900c2d6…`, then applied + built — 0 errors, Output == x64 == x86 sha `44d4b97473ec69e9…`, the UVR literals + retained s47-s76 markers verified in the deployed binary; pre-build DLL backup `20260731-192628` → restore `py tools/restore_memoria_dll.py 20260731-192628`).** WHY: U1 cast 2 proved a +128-texel u displacement on ef038 (0.84) with the labelling at 0.68 and the v axis unresolved; the impact scoping gated any kit modelling behind THIS instrument. One file (`Memoria/Battle/SFX/SfxMeshProbe.cs`), 5 hunks, additive-only, rides `CapturePrims`: a NEW rectangular row type **`UVR,effectId,frame,index,keyHex,src,prims,uMin,uMax,vMin,vMax,tpage,clut`** — one row per TEXTURED MESH row, joined 1:1 on (effectId,frame,index,keyHex); per-(key,frame) MIN/MAX of the primitives' OWN u,v bytes, accumulated in `LogPrim` BEFORE the `PrimSummary` early-return and the `PrimCap` drop (either placement below would truncate SILENTLY — the dangerous direction) and emitted beside the MESH row in `LogFrame`. Offsets per struct from `PSX_LIBGPU.cs` (clut +14 everywhere; tpage **+22 on FT / +26 on GT** — the per-vertex rgbc words shift GT; a generic table would misread half the textured population); keys mirror `SFXRender.Add()`'s dispatch exactly (36/44/52/60 plain, 76 `\|FILTER_POINT`, 68/72 `\|FILTER_BILINEAR\|FULL_BLUR_TXTURE`, SPRT 100/116/124 via the current-page key with the DISPATCH w/h span) — the two key builders are VERBATIM MIRRORS of `SFXKey.cs:19-45` **minus the `tmpABR` write** (`SFXMesh.cs:85` reads it; an instrument must not poke shared render state). Accumulator: pass-stamp reuse, ~20 entries ever, zero steady-state GC, repeat-Render() re-emits correctly. Confirm on a section DESCENDS the group ladder into the checklist (parent held secondary -- this is what makes CLICKING a section do something); Cancel walks back up one tier; an unreached section refuses with the stock buzz. The registry parses E records (18 columns) alongside S; entries travel with their sections on the whole-document replace. Gates: reverse `-R -F0 --binary` clean on live; forward `-F0 --binary` clean on a fresh base+stack replay (s63 skipped -- retired history). One file: `Global/JournalUI.cs`. **M3 (`--label s82-journal-m3`, owner-confirmed 'looks good'): the SIDE QUESTS tab** -- L1/R1 page the tabs (SFX 1047, caption rail renames, MuteActiveSound across the rebuild); the 11 side arcs listed by first availability, SC-gated ????; the detail pane renders COUNTER rows from the kit's C records (the DLL implements five primitive reads -- byte/bit/bitcount/field/sysvar -- the kit names every offset). **M4 (`--label s82-journal-m4`): the RECORDS tab** -- three always-open groups (Tetra Master / Party / Combat & Meta) through the same pane via rec.* C records whose kinds call the ENGINE'S OWN statics (QuadMistDatabase.MiniGame_Get*, party.gil, rare_item_obtained.Count, Settings.time h/m, FieldEntrance u16). **M5 (`--label s82-journal-m5`, backup 20260901-203940, UNPLAYTESTED): the missable column + polish** -- LAW 4 rendered (a closed-window uncollected row wears FF9TextTool.Red and its help bubble leads with the verdict: 'Window likely closed' at derived confidence, 'PERMANENTLY MISSED' only at owner confidence, nothing otherwise -- `JournalUI.MissableVerdict` mirrors `journalcatalog.missable_verdict`, E-record cols 13/14); a reached section row shows its own N/M tally; L1/R1 glyphs (FF9UIDataTool.ButtonGameObject, the live binding) flank the tab name on the caption rail. **M5 fix 1 (`--label s82-journal-m5-fix1`): the red rows + verdict bubble OWNER-CONFIRMED; the L1/R1 glyph hint REMOVED (rendered as empty boxes over the list; ruled non-stock), and section-row labels shrink-to-fit so the tally is never clipped. Fix 3 (`--label s82-journal-m5-fix3`): still spilling -- the row tally is REMOVED (owner: no counter on the selector list) and the donor label is left exactly as baked; the section list is the M4 state again.** |

| `s91-sibling-afterbuild-nodeploy.patch` | **`--no-deploy` DEPLOYS NOTHING NOW — the two sibling AfterBuild targets get s45's `DWIXNoDeploy` condition (applied to the shared clone 2026-09-26; build infrastructure only: a csproj edit never reaches a compiled DLL, so the bundle bytes are unaffected).** THE LEAK: s45 gated only `Assembly-CSharp.csproj`'s `AfterBuild`, but msbuild builds its ProjectReferences first, and `Memoria.Prime.csproj` + `UnityEngine.UI.csproj` each carry their OWN unconditional `<Target Name="AfterBuild"><Deploy/>`. `Memoria.MSBuild/Deploy.cs` finds the game through the Steam registry and `File.Copy`s the `.dll` and a freshly pdb2mdb-generated `.dll.mdb` into BOTH arches' Managed folders, unbacked. So every `build_memoria.py --no-deploy` compile check rewrote the live `Memoria.Prime.dll.mdb` + `UnityEngine.UI.dll.mdb` with a new sha each run (observed 2026-09-26). The DLL bytes happened to match, so behavior never changed, but a compile check of an in-flight `Memoria.Prime` edit (s56 edits `Log.cs`) or a `UnityEngine.UI` edit would have pushed it live. The same hole sat under the release procedure's "proof worktree with the auto-deploy gated off": that build compiles the siblings too. The fix is one line per file: `<Target Name="AfterBuild" Condition="'$(DWIXNoDeploy)' != 'true'">`. **Why a csproj gate and not `-p:BuildProjectReferences=false`:** that flag stops msbuild from COMPILING the siblings, so a compile check of a `Log.cs` edit would link Assembly-CSharp against the stale `Output\Memoria.Prime.dll` and prove nothing (and it fails on a fresh tree with no Output). The gate keeps all three compiled and only skips the copy, and it holds for a raw `-p:DWIXNoDeploy=true` msbuild run as well as for the wrapper. **The property reaches the siblings, calibrated before the edit:** three scratch projects on this MSBuild (a parent with a gated and an ungated ProjectReference, each `AfterBuild` a `Message`). With `-p:DWIXNoDeploy=true` only the ungated child fired, printing `DWIXNoDeploy='true'`, which reproduces the leak and proves the property arrives. Without the flag all three fired. **The wrapper now enforces it at the call site** (`tools/build_memoria.py`): `--no-deploy` walks Assembly-CSharp's ProjectReference closure and REFUSES unless every `<Deploy>` task in it is gated (on the pre-s91 clone it named exactly the two siblings; XInput's commented-out AfterBuild is correctly ignored). It then sha-fingerprints all 12 live engine files (each DLL + its `.mdb`, both arches) before msbuild and again after, on the FAILURE path too, because a sibling deploys before Assembly-CSharp compiles. Any drift exits 4, names each file, and keeps the drifted DLLs' pre-run bytes under the canonical backup name (`restore_memoria_dll.py <ts>` puts them back). The "NOTHING deployed" line is printed only after that comparison passes. **End-to-end on the real clone** (`py tools/build_memoria.py --no-deploy`, 0 errors, 8 s): both siblings really recompiled (their Output `.dll`/`.pdb` re-stamped), their Output `.dll.mdb` did NOT (pdb2mdb is the Deploy task's first step, so the task never ran), and all 12 live files matched an independent sha + mtime listing taken before the run. ⚠ Because the csproj is an input, that first build recompiled both siblings. The next DEPLOYING build therefore pushes byte-new, source-identical sibling DLLs, covered as always by its backup + verify. Gates: pre-edit `diag` over both csprojs `==` (`2 of 2`; no earlier patch names them, so the baseline is base `6b8bb2d5`). Full-file audit before the edit `87 of 87 (1 EOL-matched)`, over the `+++` paths of every live patch with the in-flight `s90-harness-facing.patch` (branch `claude/story-trace`, already in the clone) `--insert-after s89`. Captured with `emit --files Memoria.Prime/Memoria.Prime.csproj,UnityEngine.UI/UnityEngine.UI.csproj`: 1,142 bytes, 2 files, CRLF kept, 1 no-newline marker (`Memoria.Prime.csproj` has no final newline). Forward `git -c core.autocrlf=false apply --check` + `patch --binary -p1 -F0 --dry-run` exit 0 onto the `snapshot` baseline. Baseline + s91 is byte-equal to live on both files. `git apply -R --check` against the clone exits 0. `diag` with s91 gives `2 of 2` byte-exact; the control with `--skip s91` gives `0 of 2`. Full-file audit with s91 `89 of 89 (1 EOL-matched)`: both csprojs JOIN the audit set. Blobs: `Memoria.Prime.csproj` `1013c42` → `96885f2`, `UnityEngine.UI.csproj` `bc402d5` → `6a6f234`. Tests: `ff9mapkit/tests/test_build_memoria.py` 17 passed, including a pin on this patch's two lines and a skip-if-absent check that the shared clone gates every Deploy it builds. Three mutants of the wrapper (a root-only gate read, a DLL-only fingerprint, a success-path-only tripwire) each turn it red. ⚠ Numbering: `s90` is taken by `claude/story-trace` (not yet on master), so this is `s91`. |
| `s92-worldmap-custom-form-cells.patch` | **STORY TERRAIN ON ANY CELL (terrain study P1; the forms lane's F15 sketch).** Stock switches 26 baked cells between two block forms; every other cell is `IsSwitchable = false` with no form-2 parts, so no data file could switch it. New `Memoria/World/CustomFormCells.cs` (+ csproj): `WMWorld.Initialize` ARMS every non-stock cell that has a loose `Block[x][y] Terrain2.ff9mesh` in the active override namespace (`IsSwitchable` set at runtime -- no new serialized field: WMBlock/WMWorld are baked); `LoadBlock` registers an armed cell's form-1 terrain a second time under the child name `Terrain2` (the s34 key, so the loose file replaces it, render and walk; `RegisterBlockComponent` gains an optional `name`) and keeps its form-1 Object (or bare kit Object) in form 2 too, unless a loose `Object2` replaces it; the end of `ff9.w_worldChangeBlockSet` switches an armed cell to form 2 when its `Block[x][y] Form.txt` NCalc condition holds (evaluated like an Environment.txt `[Condition=...]`; none or a throw = form 1, logged). The 26 stock cells are never touched. IDENTITY-SAFE: no Terrain2 on a non-stock cell = nothing armed, stock behaviour; the live install had none when this landed (`world-forms --stack`). Logs `[CustomFormCells] armed ...` per armed cell and `... switched to form 2` per switch. Captured with `memoria_stack_replay.py emit`; gated: reverse-apply clean, full-stack diag byte-exact on every patched file. **IN-GAME PROVEN** (terrain study round 6, 12/12): cell (6,12) switched on `true` and on a story flag, stayed stock on `false`/flag off/no `Form.txt`, and its Object stayed walkable in form 2. |
| `s93-worldmap-custom-form-objects.patch` | **A STORY BUILDING ON ANY CELL (terrain study, after P1).** s92 read a loose `Object2` only on a cell with a stock Object, so a building could not appear in form 2 on a cell with none, and a bare kit `Object` stayed in both forms. In `WMWorld.LoadBlock`: an armed custom cell's loose `Object2` counts on a BARE cell too, where it registers through `RegisterBareObjectOverride` (now `form1`/`form2`/`name` parameters) in form 2 only -- render only, like the bare Object; a bare `Object` beside it is form 1 only, the rule a stock Object already had. "Object2" maps to the OBJECT-atlas material (`WMBlock` MaterialDatabase). IDENTITY-SAFE: nothing changes without an `Object2` on an armed cell. Kit side: `world-forms --building2`. Captured with `memoria_stack_replay.py emit`; gated: reverse-apply clean, full-stack diag 91/91 byte-exact. **IN-GAME PROVEN** (terrain study round 7, 15/15): a beacon `Object2` on bare cell (7,12) rendered in form 2 only and its footprint blocked the walk, on discs 1 and 4. |

## The AUTOMATED TEST HARNESS — NEW capability

| File | What it adds |
|---|---|
| `s83-harness-agent.patch` | **THE IN-GAME TEST HARNESS — the agent half of an automated play/verify loop (★ DEPLOYED + PLAYTEST-PROVEN 2026-08-27: an injected 120-frame `hold up` walked Zidane 1014 units across bench 30801, owner-witnessed and screenshot-confirmed; the driver half is `tools/harness/` + `tools/play.py`, guide in `ff9mapkit/docs/TEST_HARNESS.md`).** Lifts the project's oldest hard constraint — *the agent cannot play the game*. Before this, a change could be built, deployed and screenshotted (`tools/game_snap.ps1`) but never *exercised*: no button could be pressed, the character's position was unknown, and on-screen dialogue was unreadable, so every behavioural claim cost a human playtest. New `Memoria/Harness/HarnessAgent.cs` (+ csproj include): a `DontDestroyOnLoad` MonoBehaviour bootstrapped once per frame from `UIKeyTrigger.Update` (alive from the TITLE screen onward, so a scenario can drive New Game, not just an already-loaded field). It reads a line-oriented `ff9harness/req.txt`, publishes `ff9harness/state.json` every other frame, appends `events.jsonl`, and captures PNGs into `shots/`. **Input is injected at FIVE points in `HonoInputManager`** — `IsInput`/`IsInputDown`/`IsInputUp` (the choke point every consumer reads: field, world, battle, menus), plus `GetAxis` and `CheckPersistentDirectionInput` — so the harness needs **no window focus**, does not steal the keyboard, and OR-s with real input so a human can drive the same session by hand. **DORMANT BY DEFAULT and that is load-bearing, not politeness:** with no `ff9harness/arm` file `HarnessAgent.Active` is a false static, the three hooks early-out on one predictable branch, nothing is written, and behaviour is identical to an unpatched engine — which is what makes it safe on an install shared by ~26 concurrent worktrees. **Five design points that are the difference between a harness and a flake generator.** (1) **Absolute-frame scheduling**: Unity does not order component Updates, so a press scheduled for "now" could be sampled by `HonoInputManager` either side of our own Update and be seen once, twice or never; every schedule is an absolute `Time.frameCount` decided at least one frame ahead, so all consumers in a frame agree regardless of order. (2) **The ack is latched on PERSISTENT state, not a frame-local "was the queue busy"** — when the final step blocks (press, wait, warp, shot: almost all of them) the queue empties one frame before the block elapses and a frame-local flag is already false, losing the ack forever and hanging every command until the driver's timeout. Caught by the offline stand-in before it ever cost a game launch. (3) **`WriteAtomic` swallows the sharing violation instead of retrying** — Windows fails the replace whenever the driver has `state.json` open, a real collision at 30 publishes/sec against a 30 Hz poll; spinning to win a file race inside the frame loop would stutter the very game being measured, and skipping is free because the next publish is two frames away and the driver retries torn reads. (4) **Warping is NOT reimplemented** — `Ff9mkDebugMenu` gains three thin `Harness*` adapters that push arguments into the same text boxes a human types into and call the same private methods the buttons call, so there is exactly one implementation of the proven fade-then-change-map sequence (`ServicePendingWarp` runs from `Update()` whether or not the menu is open, so a harness warp completes with no window ever shown). (5) **THE KEY BOOLEANS ARE NOT ENOUGH — the harness must drive the ANALOG AXIS too, and this cost the arc's only failed playtest round.** With `[AnalogControl] Enabled=1` (the shipped default) `FieldMapActorController.MovePC` reads `analogVector = GetAxis()` and ends with `if (analogVector.magnitude <= stickThreshold) actualMoveVec = Vector3.zero;`. `GetAxis()` returns **Unity's own** Horizontal/Vertical axes, which a physical keyboard feeds and an injected press does not — so with only the three `IsInput` hooks the axis stayed `(0,0)` and the move vector was zeroed *downstream of every signal a diagnostic could reach*: `movingUp` true, `ccSMoveKey` true, `GetUserControl()` true, `dash_inh` 0, and the actor even **rotated** (that block is gated on `movingUp`, not on the move vector). Six benches all read as "not walkable" and the harness looked correct at every probe point. `HarnessAgent.TryGetAxis` now synthesises a unit vector from the held directions (falling through to the real axis when the harness holds nothing, so a human still drives an idle session), and `CheckPersistentDirectionInput` — the engine's raw `GetAsyncKeyState` "is a direction key physically down" probe — answers truthfully for harness-held directions so injected input reads as a **keyboard, not a stick**. That second hook is not cosmetic: `FieldMapActorController` derives `isStickMovement` from it, and with `UseAbsoluteOrientation=3` (walkpath for keys, absolute for sticks) a scenario would otherwise walk a *different direction* than the same press does by hand. **The general law: hooking the accessor a diagnostic reads proves nothing about the value that decides the outcome.** Also adds `TitleUI.HarnessStartNewGame` — a scenario could blind-press Confirm on the title, but the highlighted entry depends on whether a save exists, so the same script would start a new game on one machine and resume someone's save on another. Every state read is individually try/caught and degrades to `null`: the file is sampled at the title, mid-transition and mid-battle, where half these singletons do not exist. Five files touched (`HonoInputManager.cs`, `UIKeyTrigger.cs`, `Ff9mkDebugMenu.cs`, `TitleUI.cs`, the csproj) plus one new. <br><br>**★★★ REVISION 4 (2026-08-31, protocol `v` 3 -> 4) — THE BATTLE IS PLAYABLE, not merely visible (★ `scenarios/battle_play.py` 9/9 and `scenarios/flee_check.py` 4/4 in-game, both core-suite members).** rev 3 could SEE a fight and not take one turn in it. Two obstacles were named then; **one of them was wrong and the correction is the more valuable half.** ❌ NOT an ATB deadlock: the claim was that `btl_sys.CheckEscape` needs `IsNativeEnableAtb()` and WAIT mode freezes the gauge while a menu is open, but that method returns `CurrentPlayerIndex == -1 || ActiveGroup == "Battle.Command" || ActiveGroup == ""` — the TOP-LEVEL command list, which is exactly where the trace found the cursor, leaves it ENABLED (only a SUBMENU freezes it). The gate was open the whole time. ★ **THE REAL CAUSE WAS A ONE-FRAME HOLE IN THE HARNESS'S OWN HOLD**: `Schedule` set `_downFrame = frameCount + 1`, so re-issuing a hold un-pressed the button for the frame the request landed on — invisible to anything SAMPLING it, fatal to anything counting CONTINUOUS held time. `BattleHUD._runCounter` is exactly that and gates the escape roll at 1.0 UNBROKEN seconds, so a flee re-issued every 0.8s NEVER ROLLED ONCE while `btl_escape_key` (set before the counter is tested) kept the character running on screen — owner-observed as "the flee animation for a couple of seconds but he didn't actually leave". New `Extend` lengthens a live hold instead of restarting it. ★ The OTHER obstacle was real and dissolved by the UI's own teardown: `SendNetCommand` opens `if (playerIndex == CurrentPlayerIndex) return false;` (it replays a REMOTE co-op command and must lose a race against the local menu), and **`SetIdle()` is public** — what `OnTargetConfirm` calls the instant a command is sent — so calling it FIRST hands the slot back; the menu had to close anyway. ⚠ **THE STALE TURN, found by the SUITE and invisible to two green standalone runs:** `CurrentPlayerIndex`/`ReadyQueue`/`InputFinishList` are reset by `InitialBattle()`, which runs LATER than `IsBattleScene()` goes true — so through the opening camera of the SECOND battle in a session they hold the PREVIOUS fight's contents and `turn.slot` publishes a plausible "your move" for a battle asking nobody. `NetMenusReady` cannot catch it either (it is a ContainsKey, and InitialBattle clears the dict's VALUES not its KEYS). A command injected there FROZE the fight: no HUD, no ATB, the intro camera held four minutes. Gated now on **`FF9BMenu_IsEnable()`** — `turn.slot`/`ready`/`done` publish through it with `slot_raw` kept for diagnosis, and `battlecmd` + `menus` refuse a battle that is not asking. New: **`menus <slot>`** (the co-op mirror `CollectNetMenus`, published as `battle.menu` — commands / abilities / items with names, MP, enabled and the exact `sub` argument; ⚠ a VERB not a publish field, because collecting it WRITES the HUD's ability cache and an instrument may not mutate what it observes; stamped with slot+epoch so a stale read is visible), **`battle.escaping`** (`cmd_status` bit 0 — the roll actually WON, as opposed to `escape_held`, which only means the bumpers are down; between them is a dice roll of `200 / avgEnemyLevel * avgPlayerLevel / 16` percent per second), and `DescribeSlotRefusal` naming which of SendNetCommand's three tests said no. ★ **THE WHOLE REVISION TOUCHES ONE FILE**: every seam it needed — `SetIdle`, `SendNetCommand`, `CollectNetMenus`, `NetCommandForMenu`, `NetFirstAbilitySub`, `NetMenusReady`, `FF9BMenu_IsEnable` — was already public, most of it built for co-op in s37. No hunk lands in `BattleHUD.*`, so s83 and s37 never meet.<br><br>**★★ REVISION 3 (2026-08-31, protocol `v` 2 -> 3) — THE BATTLE BLOCK, the pillar the state channel was 100% dark on (★ 14/14 in-game, `scenarios/battle_check.py`, a core-suite member).** Deferred out of rev 2 on a stated precondition — *adding it without extending the stand-in reproduces the menu lane's defect at ten times the surface* — now met. Each field is written around the trap it carries, because almost everything in `FF9Battle` is AMBIGUOUS or STALE rather than absent, which makes a plausible WRONG answer the default failure mode: **`epoch`** (`party.battle_no`, the ONLY unambiguous battle-started edge; monotonic and save-persistent, so only deltas mean anything); **`result`** (⚠ 0 both DURING a battle and BEFORE any has ever run, and it persists afterwards — meaningless without the epoch); **`active` + `debug`** (⚠ `IsBattleScene()` is true for the DIORAMA too, which runs under `isDebug` where the auto-end is suppressed and the fight CAN NEVER END, so a result assertion there is green having observed nothing); a **unit roster** with HP published TWICE — logical `hp` and raw `hp_raw`, because `CurrentHp` routes through `btl_para.GetLogicalHP`, which subtracts 10000 for a `FLG_NON_DYING_BOSS` enemy under `CustomBattleFlagsMeaning=1`, so the HUD shows one value while the AI's B_MEMBER (36)/(35) reads the other; **`alive`** as the Death STATUS BIT rather than `hp == 0` (a DeathChanger unit sits at 0 HP alive, and that is the HUD's own liveness test); **`name` + `name_raw`**, the same rendered-vs-source split the dialogue needed, found again when an enemy came back as `[STRT=27,1]Fang[ENDN]`; plus ATB, MP, level, targetable, status, the command cursor by GROUP and BUTTON, `scene_info.runaway`, the battle message and the rewards. ⚠ **`phase`/`units`/`turn` are MID-BATTLE ONLY** — the engine leaves them holding the previous fight's contents, so publishing them on a field would hand a scenario a complete, plausible, entirely historical battle. ⚠ **`BattleUnit.GetIndex()` IS NEVER CALLED**: it is `while (1 << index != Data.btl_id) ++index;` with no bound, so a `btl_id` of 0 spins forever inside the state publisher and hangs the very game the harness exists to observe — the slot is computed with a bounded loop instead. Verbs: **`battle <sceneId> [group]`**, which is the field-warp adapter with `nextMode = 2`, since `SetBattleScene` (what the `.eb` ENCOUNT opcodes call) and the public `SetNextMap` are the same method (`FF9ChangeMap`) — so there is no second copy of a proven transition; and **`battlecmd`** onto `BattleHUD.SendNetCommand`. ⚠ Deliberately NOT `NetSyncDiorama.Boot`, which is cheaper and sets `isDebug`. **DRIVING a battle to a RESULT was left OPEN here and is CLOSED in rev 4** — see that row. ⚠ The two obstacles recorded in this row were half wrong: the ATB-deadlock reading of `IsNativeEnableAtb()` is FALSE (it returns true for the top-level `Battle.Command` group, which is where the cursor was), and the real cause was a one-frame hole in the harness's own re-issued hold. The `SendNetCommand` half was right, and `SetIdle()` dissolves it. At this revision the harness could SEE a battle in full and not play one.<br><br>**★★ REVISION 2 (2026-08-31, protocol `v` 1 -> 2) — BUILT, DEPLOYED AND PROVEN IN-GAME (49/49 checks across ten scenarios; sha `217410de06c089ca…`, Output == both arches, 0 errors; pre-build backups `20260831-144736` and `20260831-150435`). A DEFENSIVE BATCH: eleven changes, nine of which stop the agent LYING, corrupting its own channel, or acking a step it did not perform.** From an adversarial audit of both halves (51 confirmed defects after a refutation pass). **E11 THE SAVE SANDBOX is the one that matters most and it is MEASURED, not suspected:** `scenarios/save_untouched.py` ran against a live game and an ordinary `newgame(); warp()` — the opening EVERY scenario shares — changed both live save containers, because `EventEngine` autosaves on field entry and `Configuration.SaveFile.DisableAutoSave` is 0 here. Manual slots survived; the autosave did not. While armed, `SharedDataBytesStorage.MetaData.DirPath`/`.FilePath` now point into `ff9harness/save/` (redirecting `FilePath` moves BOTH containers — the Memoria sidecar path derives from it), restored on disarm and on a fault, retried every frame until it takes (the arm transition can land before the save statics resolve), and **published as `save_path`/`save_sandboxed` so the driver VERIFIES the redirect instead of trusting it** — a sandbox nobody checked is a check that cannot fail. **E3 no partially built section can reach `state.json`:** `-1 >> 3` is `-1` in C# too, so a negative watch bit passed the old shifted bound test and threw on the array read with its key already in the shared buffer; the catch did not roll back, so every later document was invalid JSON — which the driver reads as *the agent never published*, i.e. *this engine predates s83*, about a perfectly healthy game, and a re-arm did not clear it. Each section now builds into its own `StringBuilder` and is appended only on success, and the bound is tested on `n`. **E4 `texts` is now the RENDERED dialogue** (`CurrentParser.ParsedText`), with the source kept alongside as `phrase_raw`: `Dialog.Phrase` is `InitialText` — raw sub-page source, every `[STRT=…]`/`[TIME=…]` tag intact, before variable substitution — so `expect_text` was matching a string the player never sees. **E5 the choice publishes `active`/`disabled`:** a script-disabled line is physically REMOVED from the parsed text while `SelectChoice` still counts the ABSOLUTE list, so `options()[i]` was not the option `select(i)` lands on and a story scenario could confirm one branch while reporting green for another. Nothing outside the engine can reconstruct that mapping. **E6 refusals throw:** `HarnessWorldWarp`/`HarnessWorldTeleport` (and `WorldWarp`/`WorldTeleportTo`) return `Boolean`, and `HarnessStatus` — which existed and was read by nothing — is published as `debug_status`; seven refusal paths used to ack CLEAN while doing nothing, after which the driver timed out blaming a destination the engine never attempted. **E1 the error latch is per-REQUEST** and stamped with `error_seq` (it was cleared only on re-arm, so one bad warp made every later healthy step raise on a stale message — a sweep reported every remaining field as unreachable). **E2 `release` cannot press:** it wrote only `_upFrame`, and `ClearAllButtons` leaves `_downFrame` at `-1`, so `f >= -1 && f < f+1` made a defensive release inject a one-frame phantom press and a phantom cursor step. **E7 `timescale` is clamped to 0.1–8 and restored on disarm/quit:** at 0 the engine runs zero LOGICAL ticks while `Block`/`IsHeld`/the ack keep advancing on render frames, so every step acks against a game that executed nothing — and nothing restored the scale, leaving the SHARED install frozen after a crashed run. **E8 disarm and fault are observable and TERMINAL:** `armed` is published, the disarm branch emits a final document saying so (otherwise *disarmed*, *hung* and *slow* are indistinguishable), and `Update`'s catch now latches `_faulted` — before, a fault disarmed, dropped the in-flight request, released every held button mid-action, then silently re-armed 30 frames later having wiped the error that explained it. **E9 the `reset` verb** — the isolation primitive: `hold` is non-blocking and frame-counted, so a scenario that raises mid-hold left a button DOWN into the next one, and buttons were only cleared on an arm transition. **E10 publishes the menu `group` and `ButtonGroupState.ActiveButton`:** `ui_state` does not move for a menu SUB-screen, so nothing published could verify a menu was entered or LEFT — and `UIKeyTrigger` reads `ActiveButton` first while the harness was publishing the `UICamera.selectedObject` fallback. **Deliberately LEFT OUT:** the whole battle state block and a `battle <sceneId>` verb (ten times the surface, and two crash-class reads sit in the obvious implementation), a `noencounter` booster, and driving `Block` off a logical-tick counter. New in-game acceptance: `scenarios/rev2_proof.py`, every check on an OUTCOME (the character did not move, the channel is still parseable, the position is unchanged) because *the step acked* is what all of these defects could already fake. **E12 `Application.Quit()` DOES NOT QUIT THIS GAME — found by running it, not by reading it.** Every harness run ended `quit did not land -- terminating`: the step was accepted, executed and ACKED at frame 842, and the agent was still publishing 900 frames later. `UIKeyTrigger.OnApplicationQuit` calls `Application.CancelQuit()` unless its private `quitConfirm` is set, and pops the in-game "really quit?" dialog instead — which nobody answers in an unattended run. The agent now calls the game's own `UIKeyTrigger.ConfirmQuit()` (sets the flag, broadcasts `"OnQuit"`, then quits through the branch that actually closes the window), falling back to a bare `Quit()` where there is no `UIKeyTrigger` to cancel it. **A run is 23s instead of 38s** and the harness no longer hard-kills the process it opened. Worth keeping: the driver's message was ACCURATE the whole time and nobody read it. **In-game proof:** `scenarios/save_untouched.py` ★ RED → GREEN (all 18 containers byte-identical after a full `newgame(); warp(); walk`), `rev2_proof.py` 12/12, `dialogue_render.py` 6/6 — E4 shown on one box in both forms (`rendered '== SCRIBE CONTROL…'` vs `source '[STRT=10,1][TAIL=UPR][NTUR]== SCRIBE CONTROL…'`) and E5 at the exact historical failure point (`option_index("Tetra Master") -> 3`, the engine's own `SelectChoice -> 3`, the option there reads `Tetra Master`; asking for index 3 against the RAW array reads "Minigames") — plus the pre-existing suite unbroken (walk 2/2, flag 7/7, goto 5/5, menu-pick 6/6, talk 3/3, cutscene 4/4, gateway 3/3). Gates: `git apply -R --check` clean on the live tree, AND forward-apply onto the reconstructed baseline reproduces all 8 files BYTE-EXACTLY — reverse alone is not enough, it once silently passed a patch that had dropped the `WorldWarp` void→Boolean conversion. <br><br>**★ REVISION 5 (2026-09-04, protocol `v` 4 → 5) — THE `netsync` VERBS: the F1–F3 co-op solo benches, run UNATTENDED (★ `scenarios/coop_dialogue_lockstep.py` 40/40 in-game — the first run of F3's benches ever).** The benches were ~ debug-menu buttons — IMGUI, which no injected controller input can press. `netsync selftest 1|0` · `bench 1|0` · `l1 1|0` · `advance` · `choice <i>` · `unmatched` call the SAME static entry points the buttons call (`NetSyncClient.HarnessInjectDialog` → the bench Impl; `Ff9mkDebugMenu.HarnessFrontmostOpenDialog`), every refusal carries the engine's own reason, and a new `netsync` state block publishes the L2 observables (enabled/role/selftest/forced/bench/l1/l1_pinned/l1_forced_control/suppress/align_win/align_text/applied_seq/wait_armed/wait_ms/wait_limit_ms/pending). **`selftest 1` is a PROCESS-LOCAL override (`NetSyncClient.HarnessSelfTest`)**: it sets the statics `LoadConfigIfChanged` would for `Enabled=1 Role=selftest` and creates the object as `Ensure()` would — Memoria.ini is never written (the shared install; a leaked ini edit would hand the next player a ghost); the mtime-gated reload never re-reads an unchanged ini so the override holds; a live host/client session is REFUSED; a genuine ini edit mid-run takes precedence. Released by `ReleaseNetsyncOverride()` on disarm, fault and `reset` (the same teardown a live `Enabled=0` edit takes — proven in-game: `reset` with L1 pinning control hands control back with co-op disabled). `NetSyncClient.cs` joins the patch's file set (the entry points + observables sit between the F3 bench Impl and the F1 teleport section, so this revision applies AFTER `s84`). Two bench laws the run taught: a `[NFOC]`/`[TIME]` window ignores `OnKeyConfirm` by design and a real host never emits for one — never fabricate a frame for it; and a still-typewriting window eats the first Confirm as a fast-forward. Regenerated via `tools/memoria_stack_replay.py`: 19 hunks, 9 sections (the csproj hunk carried verbatim); forward onto the pre-s83 baseline (base→…→s57→s84→…→s83-keyitem) under BOTH `git apply --check` and `patch -p1 -F0 --dry-run`; `git apply -R --check` on live clean; the full replay reproduces every file byte-exactly. Live DLL `a2d69edd057d982f…`, pre-build backup `20260904-163811`. |
| `s88-story-trace.patch` | **THE STORY-WRITE TRACE — which script wrote which story byte, recorded while the game plays (★ **DEPLOYED LIVE + rung 0 in-game PROVEN 11/11** (`story-rung0`): built by `build_memoria.py --label pre-s88-story-trace`, 0 errors, live sha `c55377f6c137d442…`, pre-build backups `20260924-172331`; NOT yet in the shipped `dwix-custom-memoria-*.zip`, which waits for the next re-cut). EB board #3, the narrative-state arc's missing instrument. The row contract is [`../studies/story-trace/PLAN.md`](../studies/story-trace/PLAN.md) THE ROW CONTRACT (proto 1) — the engine writes it, the kit reads it; every row field below is named there.** 7 files, one new: `Memoria/Harness/StoryTrace.cs` (+ csproj include). **The hook** is `EBin.SetVariableValueInternal`, taken only when the buffer `ReferenceEquals` the live `gEventGlobal` — the one store every script write passes (every width and opcode, bit read-modify-writes, fields, battles, the world map), which also catches the one in-script bypass, the Oeilvert hotfix at `DoEventCode.cs:223`. At the store `ip` is mid-expression, so `EBin.next` latches the opcode start where it reads the opcode byte; a latch left by another object gives `ip` -1. `setVarManually`, the only non-script road into the store, flags its rows `src "cs"` (the no-encounter hacks, WorldHUD and UIKeyTrigger go through it too but write MAP variables, never story bytes). **`tag`** is the containing function: the largest `2 + offset` at or below `ip` in the entry's function table (`EventEngine.GetIP`'s layout); an addition buffer (`isAdditionCommand` / `currentByte` not the entry's `ebData`: `movQData`, `neckTurnData`) gets `tag` -1 and `add` 1. **`w`** is the engine's own `EBin.VariableType` name — `SBit` `Bit` `Int24` `UInt24` `SByte` `Byte` `Int16` `UInt16`, the kit disassembler's `VAR_TYPE` spelling — and `old`/`new` read that width exactly as `GetVariableValueInternal` does (UInt24's sign-extending read included; never by calling it, which would clobber the interpreter's `_v0`). `cs` and `harness` rows carry `sid`/`uid`/`lvl`/`ip`/`tag` -1: at a C# store `s1` is whatever object ran last. **The residue net** (`EventEngine.ProcessEvents`, top of every pass): the pass counter `p`, a `ReferenceEquals` array swap (New Game, save load) as a `swap` epoch, else a 2048-byte shadow compare whose differences are `r` rows `why "frame"`. A swap diffs the OLD array against the shadow first (`frame` rows stamped with its SC), so a bypass write between the last pass and the replacement is not absorbed into the epoch; the store hook and the in-place epochs take the same swap path when they are the first to see a replaced array. **THE ORDERING LAW**: before a hooked store the touched bytes are compared live vs shadow and any difference is emitted as `prestore` residue FIRST, then the write row, then the shadow takes those bytes — otherwise an earlier bypass write to the byte (or a sibling bit) is absorbed into the hooked write. The engine drops only residue on 2032-2041 (the netsync cells); script writes there are still `w` rows and everything else is raw for the kit to mask. **Suppression** per site key (`fld`,`m`,`src`,`sid`,`tag`,`ip`,`byte`,`w`,`bit`) per epoch: the first same-value store is emitted `same 1` and later ones counted, the first 64 changing writes are emitted and later ones counted; counts leave as `c` rows at every epoch and at `storytrace 0`, each stamped with its SITE's `fld`/`don`/`m` (where the stores ran; `f`/`p`/`sc` are the flush's). The field and mode are in the key because a field change opens no epoch and sibling fields run byte-identical code at one (sid, tag, ip): Dali's `Bit[2102]` store at e2 tag 1 ip 479 runs in 350/352/354/356/358 and 450, and 2509 of the census's 26603 literal (site, value) keys recur in more than one field — without them field 450's store was counted under field 350's row and no row ever named it. **Epochs** (`e`, the shadow resyncs silently, never 2048 rows): `arm`, `swap`, `debug-restore` / `debug-clear` (`Ff9mkDebugMenu`'s in-place restore and clear — the clear is also reached by a debug warp with reset), `netsync` (in `NetSyncState.ApplyStory`, not `ApplyStoryTo`, which SelfTest drives against a scratch buffer), `off`. **Control** (`HarnessAgent`): `storytrace 1|0`; arming resets the tracer to off with nothing buffered; disarm, a fault, the `reset` verb and `quit` stop it (counts, `off`, a last append); the harness's own `flag` / `byte` pokes are `src "harness"` rows that update the shadow (its `warp … SC` stamp goes through the ~ menu, so it is residue). Rows buffer in memory and append to `ff9harness/story.jsonl` once per Unity frame and at stop — invariant-culture, numbers as JSON numbers; `state.json` gains an additive `"storytrace": {"proto": 1, "on", "rows", "suppressed", "error"}` capability block (protocol `v` stays 5), so an older engine fails visibly. **Dormant = one static bool test** on the store, the opcode read, each `ProcessEvents` pass and `setVarManually`; nothing can throw into the engine (every entry point is caught; a failure logs once, turns the tracer off and publishes `error`); no field on a baked MonoBehaviour. Not visible to it (all outside `gEventGlobal`): ATE seen-state, party state, and `gScriptVector` / `gScriptDictionary` — the 0xD3 VECTOR / VECTOR_SIZE / DICTIONARY lane, where the kit keeps persistent tables, the fight ledger and roll streams: `SetVariableValue`'s `VariableSource.Null` branch writes them directly and never reaches the store point, so a fork's table writes give no row (the `debug-clear` epoch is the only trace that they were emptied). Gates: captured with `emit --skip s88-story-trace.patch` (the replay baseline is the stack through s87), then forward `git -c core.autocrlf=false apply --check` + `patch --binary -p1 -F0 --dry-run` onto the `snapshot --stop-after s87-field70-movepc-walkmesh-guard.patch` baseline (the patch is CRLF-stored: without `--binary` every CRLF hunk reports `different line endings` and exits 1), `git apply -R --check` against the clone, `diag` with s88 installed `==` on all 7 files, full-file audit `88 of 88 (1 EOL-matched)` — all re-run after the review fix pass (the site key gained `fld`/`m`; a swap diffs the old array first), which changed only `StoryTrace.cs` (blob `873cabb`). Next: `build_memoria.py --label s88-story-trace` (the backed-up deploying build), relaunch, then rung 0 on Lindblum 552 with the entrance pinned; owner decision 1 ships it in the bundle at the next re-cut beside s80/s87. |
| `s89-harness-objects.patch` | **THE HARNESS PUBLISHES THE FIELD'S OBJECTS — every other actor on the field, and what the engine's own player-vs-object collision does with it (built (compile-checked), not yet deployed: `build_memoria.py --no-deploy`, 0 errors, no new warnings, `AppendObjects` present in the Output DLL — re-run after the review fix pass, whose new string literals (`"pushout":`, `range_r`, `talk_r`) are in the freshly compiled Output DLL; DEPLOYED LIVE 2026-09-25 by `build_memoria.py --label pre-s89-harness-objects`: 0 errors, both arches sha-verified, live sha `f1fcea745aa651d2…` (s88 + s89), pre-build backups `20260925-152631`; NOT yet in the shipped bundle).** WHY: `state.json` published the player's position and no other object, so the driver's router (`Session.route_to`) planned over the walkmesh alone and found NPCs by walking into them — on the stock Dali village it had to wait, push through non-solid villagers and guess blockers, and on a tighter map a SOLID object is a true movement lock. One file, `HarnessAgent.cs` (3 hunks, +168 lines; no new `.cs`, no csproj change — it reuses the agent's own JSON writers): while ARMED and on a FIELD (`EventEngine.gMode == 1` and `SceneDirector.IsFieldScene()`), `state.json` gains an additive `"objects"` array, one entry per actor in the active object list other than the controlled player: `{"uid", "sid", "x", "y", "z", "range", "talk", "r", "solid", "coll", "range_r", "talk_r", "shown", "moving", "flags"}`, plus a sibling `"pushout"` object (below) — invariant-culture, numbers as JSON numbers; `x`/`y`/`z` are `PosObj.pos`, the same frame as `player.x/y/z` (`FieldMapActorController.HonoLateUpdate` copies `curPos` into it, `FieldMapActorController.cs:267-269`). **THE SET** is exactly what `WalkMesh.Collision` walks for the player (mode 0, `WalkMesh.cs:912-917`): actors only (`cid 4`; regions and code entries never collide), minus the player. A hidden or model-less actor still collides if its collision bit says so — an invisible wall — so it is listed with `shown` false, never dropped. **`r`** = `4*(obj.collRad + player.collRad)`: the test is `dx²+dz² − r² < −1` (`WalkMesh.cs:909`, `:928-934`; a hit must beat `minDistanceDif` 0 by more than 1, `:954`), `collRad` is `SetObjectLogicalSize`'s 2nd argument (`DoEventCode.cs:1498-1523`, default 16 at `PosObj.cs:13`), and it applies only while `|dy| < 400` (`WalkMesh.cs:922`) — the driver compares `y`. The push-out lands at `controller.radius + 4*collRad` (`FieldMapActorController.cs:781`; `radius` = 4 × the 1st argument, `DoEventCode.cs:1531`, default 96) and is REVERTED together with the move when that point is still inside `r` (`:786-791`), so `r` is the boundary either way. **`coll`**: the pair is skipped only when the PLAYER carries flag 4 AND the object flag 2 (`WalkMesh.cs:915-917`); actors are born with 4 (`Actor.cs:17`), so in practice object flag 2 = walk-through (`CFLAG 14`, the engine's own "make invisible" at `DoEventCode.cs:762`, sets 2\|4\|8); a field debug mode skips every object (`WalkMesh.cs:901-905`). ⚠ The `CFLAG` comment (`DoEventCode.cs:2073`) calls bit 2 "collision with player character" and bit 4 "collision with NPC"; read literally that is the opposite polarity (a SET object bit 2 means NO collision with a player carrying 4) — the code's pair rule is what s89 publishes. **`solid`** = `coll` and object flag 16 (`CFLAG`'s "can't walk through by insisting", `DoEventCode.cs:2073`): the colliding object sets `sLockFree = flags & 16 ? 0 : 1` and a 16 resets `sLockTimer` (`FieldMapActorController.cs:768-770`); `CheckCollFallback` (`:800-823`) adds `sLockFree` per colliding call while `SCollTimer != 0` (re-armed to 2 by each push, `:792`; decayed per event tick, `ProcessEvents.cs:302-303`) and at 25 flips it to −25 — a 25-tick window in which the push-out (`:772`, `sLockTimer >= 0`) is skipped. So a non-16 object is released after ~26 unbroken colliding `MovePC` calls and a 16 object never. `CheckCollFallback`'s own early return (effective field 2207 at SC 9840, `:802-806`) freezes the timer, so there every collidable object is `solid` — while the timer is non-negative: nothing resets `sLockTimer` on field load (its only writers are `FieldMapActorController.cs:770/811/813/817/821` and the dead `ProcessEvents.cs:176`), so a walk-through window already open on entry stays frozen open (non-16 bodies passable) until a flag-16 contact zeroes it, and `solid` there is `coll && (flags & 16 || sLockTimer >= 0)`; the agent mirrors it through a local (`effField == FallbackExemptField`) so the fork-gate censuses that grep the wrapped `EffectiveFieldId(...fldMapNo) == N` form never count a read as a gate. ⚠ `sLockFree` follows the CLOSEST colliding object, so pushing through a non-solid body wedged against a solid one may never release. **`range`** / **`talk`** = the entry HAS a tag-2 (Range) / tag-3 (talk) function (`EventEngine.GetIP` over its `ebData`, null-guarded so one entry cannot void the list). **`range_r`** = `r + speed + 60` — a TRIGGER zone, not a wall: every tick the player is under control (`ProcessEvents.cs:183-187`), standing still or not, `CollisionRequest` (`EventCollision.cs:255-297`) runs `WalkMesh.Collision` in mode 2 — the mode-0 pair rule, but only over entries with a tag-2 function (`WalkMesh.cs:917`) and with the radius grown by the object's `speed + 60` (`:930-932`) — and requests the closest hit's tag 2 (`Request` needs it idle, `level > 1`, `EventEngine.cs:339-348`; `CheckNPCPush` exempts fields 103/107 sid 3-4 and 1856 sid 5-6, not mirrored). A router that hugs `r` sets off scripts it knows nothing about: on stock field 350 (Dali/Village Road) `Vivi_Range` ends in `Field( 358 )`, so passing within 314 of Vivi (Zidane collRad 24, Vivi 32, speed 30) warps the run to the Windmill; on 555 (Lindblum/Shopping Area) `Fang_Range` starts `Battle( 0, 31 )`; stock chests carry `ChestA_Range` (347 functions across the HW exports). `null` when `coll` is false. **`talk_r`** = `4*(obj.talkRad + player.talkRad) + speed + 60`, the talk search (mode 4; `talkRad` is `SetObjectLogicalSize`'s 3rd argument; object flag 8 bars it → `null`). When that search shows the talk icon, `CollisionRequest` requests the TALK object's tag 2 instead of running mode 2 (`EventCollision.cs:270-297`), so an entry with `range` AND `talk` may also fire its Range inside `talk_r` while the player faces it (±90°). Both searches box `|dx|,|dz| < 2048`, which only a radius above 2048 can feel. The driver should keep out of `range_r` (and, for a `range`+`talk` entry, `talk_r`) unless the route means to trigger it. **`shown`** = the renderer gate (`ProcessEvents.cs:520-548`): a GameObject, flag 1, `frontCamera` (false when the projected depth leaves 100..3996, `FieldMapActor.cs:133-154`) AND at least one mesh whose `meshflags` bit `HideMesh` has not set (`PosObj.cs:95-108`; a model with no mesh list counts as shown); `null` when the GameObject carries no `HonoBehavior`, since the gate never drives its renderers (every `SetModel` actor gets a `FieldMapActor`, so that is an edge case). Not mirrored: field 52 keeps sid 6/13's renderers while hidden. **`moving`** = a scripted walk in progress (`actf & actMove`, set mid-`MoveToward` at `MoveToward.cs:270-271`, cleared by `clrdist` at `EventEngine.cs:506-510`), a controller path (`hasTarget` / `movePaths`, `MoveNPC`), or displaced this tick (the engine's own walk-animation test, `curPos` vs `lastPos`, `ProcessEvents.cs:256-260`). A walker held by a collision still reads true — scripted walkers stop on the player too (`MoveToward.cs:187-189`), so "wait for it" is a real answer. `flags` is the raw byte `solid`/`coll`/`shown` are read from. **`pushout`** = `{"slock", "scoll", "slockfree", "fallback"}` — `EventEngine.sLockTimer` / `SCollTimer` / `sLockFree` and whether `CheckCollFallback` runs here at all (false only at 2207 @ SC 9840): the push-out's GLOBAL state, which decides whether a non-solid body blocks RIGHT NOW and which no per-object bit can carry. `slock < 0` = the walk-through window is open (every non-16 body passable until it counts back to 0 or a flag-16 contact zeroes it); `slockfree` 0 while touching a non-solid villager means a flag-16 body is the closer contact. `r`/`solid`/`coll`/`range_r`/`talk_r` are relations to the player: with no controlled player they are `null` (`range`/`talk` are the entry's own). Off a field (title, battle, world) `objects` and `pushout` are both `null`, and ANY failure makes both `null` — a partial list would tell a router that a blocked path is clear. **Dormant cost nil**: `PublishState` runs only while armed; the section builds into its own buffer behind its own catch (the `-1 >> 3` law); the object walk is bounded at 512; nothing added to a baked MonoBehaviour (three `const`s on the runtime-added agent: `MaxObjectWalk`, `FallbackExemptField`, `FallbackExemptScenario`). **Protocol `v` stays 5** — `tools/harness/channel.py` bumps it only for a change in what `state.json` MEANS, and an additive capability is simply absent on an older engine (as `storytrace` in s88); the constant's comment now says so. The driver side (`tools/harness/`) is NOT in this patch: it needs a `State.objects` accessor that reads an absent key as "this engine cannot", never "nothing there". Gates: captured with `emit --files Assembly-CSharp/Memoria/Harness/HarnessAgent.cs` (the replay baseline is the stack through s88), forward `git -c core.autocrlf=false apply --check` + `patch --binary -p1 -F0 --dry-run` onto the `snapshot --stop-after s88-story-trace.patch` baseline, baseline + s89 byte-equal to live, `git apply -R --check` against the clone, `diag` with s89 installed `==` (1 of 1), full-file audit `88 of 88 (1 EOL-matched)` before the edit AND with s89 installed; LF-stored like the file, blob `0ef9497` → `94e5e57`; the stack-reading tests (`test_memoria_stack_replay`, `test_idgated`, `test_walkmesh_hotfix`, `test_fieldmapactor_tweaks`, `test_world_ledger`, `test_setup`) green. **Review fix pass** (range/talk trigger zones, mesh-aware `shown`, the frozen-window `solid`, `pushout`, these README corrections): re-emitted with `emit --skip s89-harness-objects.patch` over the same one file and every gate above re-run on the new patch — full-file audit `88 of 88 (1 EOL-matched)` before the edit and after, forward `git apply --check` + `patch --binary -p1 -F0 --dry-run` onto the s88 snapshot, baseline + s89 byte-equal to live, `git apply -R --check` on the clone, `diag` `==` (1 of 1), the tests above 110 passed. Next: `build_memoria.py --label s89-harness-objects` (the backed-up deploying build — the owner's call), relaunch, then an in-game check that a known villager's entry tracks its position, that a `CFLAG`-hidden object reports `shown` false, that Vivi on 350 reports `range` true with `range_r` 314, and that `pushout.slock` goes negative after ~26 frames pushing into a non-16 villager. |
| `s90-harness-facing.patch` | **THE HARNESS PUBLISHES THE FACING AND TURNS HIM IN PLACE — `player.yaw` / `player.face` in `state.json` and a `turn` verb (★ IN-GAME PROVEN 2026-09-27: `studies/story-trace/facing_check.py` 8/8 -- `player.face` is the kit's byte of `player.yaw`, three `turn`s moved him 0.0u and converged to the TWIST's headings to 0.001 deg, a 3-frame turn was exactly 6 whole MovePC calls, and the walker's closed loop turned him in place and crossed stock 350's gated door to 351; built (compile-checked), then DEPLOYED LIVE 2026-09-26 by the owner with `build_memoria.py --label pre-s90-harness-facing`: both arches sha-verified, live sha `ba9762423da8f3d7…` (s88 + s89 + s90), pre-build backups `20260926-171239`; the compile-check before it: `build_memoria.py --no-deploy`, 0 errors, 186 warnings = the standing count, so none new; the new literals (`turn_end`, `"face":null`, `yaw0`, every refusal message) and members (`EBin.HarnessFacingByte`, `ServiceTurn`, `BeginTurn`, `TurnBlocker`, `MoveHeld`, `TurnSettlePasses`) are in the freshly compiled Output DLL, `66e435197e999ffa…`; the live `Assembly-CSharp.dll` was then still `f1fcea745aa651d2…` on both arches (s88 + s89) — the compile-check's sibling `AfterBuild` targets did re-copy the byte-identical `Memoria.Prime.dll` / `UnityEngine.UI.dll` and freshly generated `.mdb` files into both Managed folders, a pre-existing gap in `--no-deploy`, not s90's; NOT yet in the shipped bundle).** WHY: the stock door gate (114 sites in 83 fields; Dali 350 alone has six) fires only while the player FACES the exit. It compares the byte `obj(250).f[3]` with the bearing to his projection onto the region's first edge and passes only for a signed error of −47..+47 of 256 (`<48 \|\| >208`, strict: `EBin.cs:743/756`). The driver could neither see the facing nor set it. `player.dir` is `PosObj.rot[1]`, whose only writer is the world map (`WMActor.cs:101`), so it is 0 on every field (15,484 of 15,484 archived field samples). The only way to turn him was a `hold`, which also walks him 30u per `MovePC` call, out of a small door zone or into a wall. The 23 facing misses of the Dali tour came from this ([`../studies/story-trace/HANDOFF.md`](../studies/story-trace/HANDOFF.md) THE FACING-GATE SPEC; the engine rule and the stock census: [`../studies/story-trace/research/rung3-6-the-facing-gate.md`](../studies/story-trace/research/rung3-6-the-facing-gate.md)). Two files, 12 hunks, +427/−10, no new `.cs`, no csproj change. **`EBin.cs`** (CRLF, 1 hunk, +10): a public one-liner, `HarnessFacingByte(Obj) => getvobj(obj, 3)`. That is the script read itself (`EBin.cs:1689-1691` → `getvobj` case 3, `:1811-1826`): the same gMode branch (field `((Actor)obj).rotAngle[1]`, world `wmActor.rot1`, else 0), the same private `ConvertFloatAngleToFixedPoint` (`:1273-1284`), the same `>> 4 & 255`. Type 3 only reads: `_v0` and the s88 story-trace hooks are untouched. **`HarnessAgent.cs`** (LF, 11 hunks, +417/−10): **(1) `state.json` `player` gains `yaw` and `face`** (additive, between `dir` and `floor`). `yaw` is the controlled actor's raw `rotAngle[1]` in degrees (`PosObj.cs:165`), neither unwrapped nor quantised: `MovePC`'s lerp leaves it in [−180, 180] (`FieldMapActorController.cs:759-764`), a script's `putvobj` of a byte in [0, 358.6] (`EventEngine.cs:1180-1188`). `face` is `eBin.HarnessFacingByte(controlChar)`, byte for byte what the gate reads. `GetControlChar` (`EventEngine.cs:425-431`) is the script's `obj(250)` (`:946-958`) for an actor. Both are `null` with no controlled player or off a field (`gMode == 1` and `SceneDirector.IsFieldScene()`). `dir` is unchanged. Drivers compare `face`, never a byte re-derived from `yaw`. **(2) `turn <dir>[+<dir>] [frames=30]`** (directions only, `ParseControl`'s aliases, `+`-joined; frames clamped to 1..36000; non-blocking and frame-counted like `hold`, via `Extend`, so turns issued while its keys are down compose). It presses the direction KEYS and leaves them out of the analog AXIS: `TryGetAxis` and `TryGetNavigationAxis` test `MoveHeld` = held and not a turn key. Under `[AnalogControl] Enabled=1` that is the engine's own turn in place. `movingX` comes from the keys (`FieldMapActorController.cs:612-615`, via `UIKeyTrigger.GetKey` → `IsInput`). `isStickMovement` is false (`HonoInputManager.cs:578`, `FieldMapActorController.cs:627-634`), so the target is the same 8-way direction plus twist a `hold` gets (`:698-721`). The step is zeroed because the axis is at or under the stick threshold (`:736-737`), while the facing lerp keys on the booleans (`:749-764`: 40% of the way per call). That is the state s83's missing axis produced by accident (`HonoInputManager.cs:940-950`). A turn goes through every lock a press meets: the movement mask and actor state (`:586-589`), the control mask (`:636-642`), the FieldHUD key gate (`UIKeyTrigger.cs:94-95`) and HonoUpdate's early return (`:170-173`). The number of lerps per 30 Hz tick is HonoUpdate's (`:195-209`): 2 by default, 1 with `hold cancel` or `dash_inh`. **Refusals** throw (the `error` / `error_seq` latch plus an `error` event) and press nothing: not on a field, or no player (a controlled `cid 4` actor whose controller `isPlayer`), control, movement (pad-mask or `isBattleBackupPos`), actor (`state != stateRunning`) or FieldHUD; the start unreadable; `Enabled=0` (the key path normalises and never zeroes the step, `:710-711`); any direction held or scheduled by `hold`/`press` NOW or on a later frame (the start is read this frame and the frame's tick can run after the agent, so a key down on its last frame would step and turn him after the snapshot), which for a fresh turn includes a cut turn's key still lifting; opposite directions (they cancel to a zero vector, and `atan2(−0,−0)` = −180°); a physical stick, key or virtual analog over the threshold; a pending click-to-move path (the keys clear the target and pop `movePaths` legs, `:643-657`); an overlapping collidable body (the engine's own pure mode-0 `WalkMesh.Collision`, `WalkMesh.cs:899-965`: the object push-out of a zero-step `MovePC`, `:765-793`); composing onto a turn that began on another actor or field; and a new `turn` while the last one's keys are up and its `turn_end` is not out (the field is still judging it; folding it in would swallow that `turn_end` and merge two turns). The mirror: `hold`/`press` of a direction is refused while a turn is open (its keys down, or up and being judged), or while a turn key is down on the next frame. **Lifetime**: `release <dir>` lifts a turn key by the existing rule; `reset`, disarm and a fault (`ClearAllButtons`) end it silently; `quit` ends the process. Once per armed frame after the queue, `ServiceTurn` CUTS the turn (lifting its keys by the release rule) the moment the field stops honouring it. The `why` token is one of `field`, `player`, `control`, `movement`, `actor`, `hud` or `unreadable`, plus `field` again for a new `fldMapNo` and `player` for a new controlled actor (a same-id reload, or a script handing control to another character). It is `axis` when a physical stick crosses the threshold, since he would walk. **THE FIELD JUDGES A TURN AFTER ITS KEYS LIFT**, so the report stays open until 2 event passes have run since the lift. The region pass that reads the final facing runs on a LATER pass than the `MovePC` call that wrote it: `CollisionRequest` only Requests the region's tag 2 (the code that reads `obj(250).f[3]`) after the pass's `ProcessCode` (`EventEngine.ProcessEvents.cs:122/187` → `EventCollision.cs:300-303` → `EventEngine.cs:339-349`, which only sets its ip), so it runs on the next pass. The passes are counted on `EventEngine.LastProcessTime`, the stamp every pass writes (`ProcessEvents.cs:19`) and the engine's own "has a pass run since" test (`DialogBoxSymbols.cs:836-857`); a frame that runs two passes counts once, so the wait only errs long. The same tests run during those passes and a trip reports its token: a stock door that fires runs `DisableMove` (`DoEventCode.cs:1046-1062`) in the very pass that reads the facing (Dali 350 e18: before any `Wait`), so it ends as `control`. `ended` means its keys lifted and the field ran its passes on the final facing with him still under control. `control` alone is not proof of a door (a talkable NPC's tag 2, `EventCollision.cs:269-297`, or any script can take control): confirm with the field id. As first drafted, the report came at the lift, so a door the last lerp brought into the window read `ended` and fired a tick later. **Event** `turn_end {why, frames, yaw0, yaw, face, moved}`, one per turn, about 2 ticks after the lift, so after the ack of a `wait` of frames + 2: poll for it. `why` is `ended` or the cut token. `frames` is how long the keys were down. `face` is the end byte. `moved` is the 3-D distance from the start: 0, or float noise of 0.25u or less, for a true turn in place (two walkmesh re-projections per call, `FieldMapActorController.cs:795` and `:366`, build over a turn and grow with the coordinates: a float32 emulation of a 16-call turn put the worst at 0.075u at ±16000 and 0.18u at ±30000; one step is 15-30u); anything more is a push-out, a walkmesh edge force, a human's stick or a script. The end values are `null` off a field (a battle or other non-field scene) or when the actor or field id changed. Every value but `frame` is a JSON string. While turning, `held` lists the turn keys, `input.key_up` (the only direction key published) is true for an up turn, and `input.axis_x/axis_y` are the physical axis (0). **What it does NOT do**: step him, push him (refused at start; `moved` witnesses a body walking into him), pick the walk animation or a footstep (`hasMoved` false → idle, `ProcessEvents.cs:256-287`), advance the encounter distance (`:289`), or touch collision, region or push-out state beyond what standing does. One walkmesh difference from standing is not refused, and `moved` witnesses it. MovePC's own `UpdateActiveTri` (`FieldMapActorController.cs:795`) re-projects him onto his triangle before `ServiceChar`, and that re-projection is float noise, so `ServiceChar`'s walkmesh pass (`:950-1010`) runs on it, where standing's returns at once (`:956-957`), exactly as it does for a blocked press. At a spot closer to a walkmesh edge than his radius, its edge force can move him further. `RadiusValid` / `ServiceForces` are private, so the agent cannot ask first. Everything else matches a blocked press: `ccSMoveKey` → `ResetIdleTimer` (one `Comn.random8` draw per pass, `ProcessEvents.cs:69/88-89`, `EventEngine.cs:526-531`); the player `speed` byte at 60 on a running pass (`FieldMapActorController.cs:211`, latched once control is taken); netsync counts a turn key as steering (`NetSyncClient.cs:2652-2656`); and scripts that poll the pad see the press. Facing-dependent reads change because facing changes, which is the point: the talk search and its "!" icon (`EventCollision.cs:269-283`) and every `f[3]` gate on the next pass. `face` read MID-turn is only the current value (MovePC and the tag-2 pass interleave, and state is written every 2 frames); decide from `turn_end`. The direct-set fallback (`putvobj(po, 2, byte)`) is NOT needed and not built: on this install the step is zeroed by the engine itself; keep it on record only for an `Enabled=0` install, where `turn` refuses. **Dormant cost nil**: `TryGetAxis` / `TryGetNavigationAxis` still return first on `!Active`, and with no turn `MoveHeld == IsHeld`, so every existing verb is byte-for-byte unchanged. `ServiceTurn` runs only while armed and returns at once with no turn open and no turn bit set. Every new read is behind `Try` or `TurnBlocker`'s catch (nothing can throw into the engine, or fault the agent from `Update`). All new state is `static` on the runtime-added agent; nothing is added to a baked MonoBehaviour. **Protocol `v` stays 5** (additive, like s88's `storytrace` and s89's `objects`): an older engine publishes no `yaw` / `face` key and answers `turn` with `unknown op 'turn'`, which a driver must read as "cannot". The driver side (`tools/harness/`) is NOT in this patch. Gates: pre-edit `diag` over `HarnessAgent.cs`, `EBin.cs`, `HonoInputManager.cs` and `FieldMapActorController.cs` `==` (`4 of 4 files byte-exact`). Full-file audit `88 of 88 files byte-exact (1 EOL-matched)` before the edit. Captured with `emit --files Assembly-CSharp/Memoria/Harness/HarnessAgent.cs,Assembly-CSharp/Global/EBin.cs` (baseline = the stack through s89): 33,385 bytes, 2 files, 0 no-newline markers. Forward `git -c core.autocrlf=false apply --check` and `patch --binary -p1 -F0 --dry-run` both exit 0 onto the `snapshot --stop-after s89-harness-objects.patch` baseline; without `--binary` the CRLF `EBin.cs` hunk reports `different line endings`, as in s88. Baseline + s90 is byte-equal to the live clone on both files. `git apply -R --check` against the clone exits 0. `diag --insert-after s89-harness-objects.patch=<s90>` gives `==` (`2 of 2 files byte-exact`); the same diag without it gives `0 of 2` (the control). The full-file audit with s90 inserted gives `88 of 88 files byte-exact (1 EOL-matched)`, against `86 of 88` without it; s90 adds no file to the set. Blobs: `HarnessAgent.cs` `94e5e57` → `2c9536e` (LF-stored like the file), `EBin.cs` `764a6f0` → `3aa94ae` (CRLF-stored like the file). The stack-reading tests (`test_memoria_stack_replay`, `test_idgated`, `test_walkmesh_hotfix`, `test_fieldmapactor_tweaks`, `test_world_ledger`, `test_setup`, `test_build_memoria`, `test_fork_walkmesh_lint`) pass: 164 before installing and 164 on a scratch copy of HEAD with this file and this row installed (s89's six: 110 and 110). On Dali 350, `player.face` should equal the rule's facing for the published `yaw`. `turn right 8` from a standstill should give `turn_end` `ended`, `moved` of 0.25 or less and a `yaw` converging on the right-press yaw (−88.59° there). A gated door (350 e18 → 351, pressing right from (289, −74)) should fire on a turn with no step and report `control`, then the field change to 351. `hold up` during a turn, or before its `turn_end`, should be refused, and a `turn` with the stick held should be refused or cut as `axis`. |

**THE MODEL IS DECIDED: `s12`/`s18`/`s21` are DEAD — never applied.** (User call, 2026-07-16.) They are
three early drafts of one feature: s18 = "F6 reloads the field" (in `FieldMap.cs`); s21 = the same idea moved
to `UIKeyTrigger.cs` because the first spot missed keypresses, plus F10 = reset flags; **s22 = the finished
tabbed F6 menu that replaced both**. `s12` is an unrelated grab-bag (an overlay texture cache that later
*became* `s35`, a retired New-Game debug shortcut, and the `Attack9999` auto-cheat).

Why the question existed at all: later patches carried instructions to **undo** them (s37 removed s21's F6/F10
block and s12's `Attack9999`), so the stack added code and then took it back out — **the engine is identical
either way**, and the only question was whether the recipe takes the detour. Dead wins on evidence: applying
s12 **collides with s35** (it still contains all 14 of s35's lines byte-for-byte), it would resurrect the
retired New-Game debug, and nothing in s22 removes s18's F6 hotkey (so the raw hotkey and the menu would both
grab F6). The fix was to strip the orphaned removals from s37 — done, and that is what closed the stack.

⚠ **Consequence — do not "restore" s12/s18/s21 casually.** They are kept for the build record ONLY. The live
set no longer contains anything that removes their code, so applying one now injects code nothing takes out.

Two rounds got here (below): round 1 (during `s38`) found the drift; round 2 (2026-07-16) captured every
*stable* gap, corrected three of round 1's diagnoses, and stripped s37.

`NetSyncSocket.cs` — s36's lines, in the F6-debug-menu patch (leaked in by an earlier regen emitted
against a live tree that already had s36). s36 adds them too, so applying s22→s36 collided and **the
live set had never replayed cleanly from base**. The two duplicate lines were removed from s22; the
stack now replays **s22..s36 at ZERO fuzz, zero rejects**. ⚠ Note `s22` *still* carries
`Memoria\World\WorldMeshOverride.cs` (s34's line) — **left in place deliberately: it is the ONLY patch
adding that Compile line**, so removing it without moving it into s34 breaks the build. Misattributed,
load-bearing, flagged.

**REPAIRED — an EOL defect in the live tree.** `EventEngine.DoEventCode.cs` was **LF** while the repo
checks out **CRLF** (`core.autocrlf=true`) — a past text-mode `patch` application rewrote it. Converted
back to CRLF (pure EOL, delta = exactly the line count, zero content change); its diff vs the
reconstructed baseline dropped from a phantom **6943-line rewrite to a real 152/138**. **LAW: apply
these patches with `patch --binary`** — plain text mode silently rewrites CRLF files as LF and seeds
exactly this drift.

**RECAPTURED (2026-07-15, round 2) — the stable drift is now in the stack.** Every file the round-1 note
listed as uncaptured/stale is captured. `base + s22..s36 + s39` replays under the documented
`patch -p1 -F0 -s -f --binary` at **zero fuzz, zero rejects, zero `.orig`**, and the four files below are
**byte-identical to live** (`cmp`):

| File | Was | Now |
|---|---|---|
